### DAT-044 – A column that is a calendar day
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** A model can say that a column is a calendar day, and the store refuses anything else in it.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-24 (PLAN-026), in front of DAT-008, whose columns are the first to need it: the day a source published a record is a day, with no time of day, and none is made up for it (CLAUDE.md §11). `DATABASE.md` has said since DAT-028 that a day without a time needs a type of its own. One new module, `motosphere.db.days`, with the column type `CalendarDay`, the fifth type that brings its check (DAT-034): TEXT in the table, in the one form `2026-11-24`, with a check named `ck_<table>_<column>_calendar_day`. A model says the column's Python type, `Mapped[date]`, and `Base` gets one entry in the list by which a Python type becomes a column type; the rule by which `Base` gives checks, and `broken_rules`, do not change. Settled in the planner's trial: the form, which is the one SQLite's own date functions and Python's `date.isoformat()` write, so that the order of the texts is the order of the days; the check, which asks three things of the text: that it has the form, that SQLite's calendar reads it and says the same text back (so the 30th of February is refused), and that it is not before the first of January of the year 1 (SQLite knows a year 0 and Python does not); that the form is asked first, because SQLite will not ask its calendar about the word `now` inside a check and fails with words of its own, which the trial found; that the calendar is asked with `IS` and not with `=`, because a check lets what is not known pass; that a time is refused with a sentence of its own, with an offset or without, although Python counts a `datetime` as a `date`, because the time of day would be dropped in silence and the day a moment falls on depends on its offset; that only text in the one form is read as a day; and that the years go from 1 to 9999. A day of which only the year or the month is known is not a day, and this type does not hold it: the table that first has such a thing settles how it is kept. No model, no table, no migration, no new package, no existing test changed. The planner's trial has 94 new tests and detects 63 of 63 new planted defects and 372 of 372 of the earlier tasks'.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. A model that says `Mapped[date]` of a column gets a text column with the check, named by the naming rule: the real SQLite file has the checks by name and letter for letter, for each such column and for no other. A model's `datetime` is still a time.
  2. A day is stored as text in the one form and comes back as the day, from the first day there is to the last; a column that may be without a value holds none or a day; a value of the model's own is stored in the form; a change is stored.
  3. What is not a `date` is refused before anything is written, with a sentence that says what it was given, and a time with a sentence of its own, with an offset or without, in a row and in a question.
  4. Text that is not a day of the calendar in the form is refused by SQLite with the name of the check, word for word, in twenty kinds, in every such column, in a row written with plain SQL and in a change; a number is refused by the check and bytes by the strict table.
  5. Only text in the one form is read as a day: fifteen things that are not are refused when read, also what Python could read as a day.
  6. Rows are found by a day, by the days before and after it, and in the order of the days; SQLite's own date functions read a stored day.
  7. `broken_rules` names such a column whose table is without the check, in the type's words, until the table has it; the rule by which `Base` gives checks, and `broken_rules`, are unchanged.
  8. No existing test changed, and all of them pass. The type check passes with no `ignore` and the lint with no `noqa`.
  9. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates and 1694 tests (backend tests 1064); on the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.
- **Tests required:** 45 new unit tests in fifteen functions, of saying and reading a day, of the check as SQLite is given it, of its name, of the type and of the entry in the list of `Base`; 49 new integration tests in seventeen functions, with a model that has three such columns and a time next to them, against a real SQLite file that is read with plain SQL, past the model; every existing test, unchanged. ### DAT-008 – Provenance on every collected record
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** Every collected record says where it came from, when, how, and how far it can be trusted (DESIGN §8): one set of columns, said once, that a model of collected records takes whole.
- **Description:** One new class, `Collected`, in `motosphere.db.models.provenance`, next to the two lists of DAT-043, which was cut out of this task on 2026-11-24 (PLAN-025). It is a subclass of `Base` that is no table itself, and a model whose records are collected is a subclass of it and not of `Base`. Its table then has eight columns after the columns the model names and before the two times: `source_id`, which refers to `source.id`; `source_address`, a web address (DAT-037); `collected_at`, a time; `published_on`, a calendar day, of the type of DAT-044, which was put in front of this task on 2026-11-24 (PLAN-026); `last_verified_at`, a time; `extraction_method`, one of the ways of collecting (`CollectionMethod`); `confidence` and `verification_status`, of the lists of DAT-043. Settled in the planner's trial: a class to be a subclass of and not a mixin beside `Base`, so that there is one parent and no order of parents to get wrong; that only the day of publishing may be without a value, and that no column gives itself a value, because a default would be a value nobody read anywhere (CLAUDE.md §11), `low` and `unverified` being the words for knowing little; that the extraction method is of the list of the ways of collecting, and is kept with the record although the source says its way too, because that can change; that `last_verified_at` is when the source was last seen to say what the row says, which for a new record is the moment it was collected, so it is never unknown; one check across two columns, `ck_<table>_last_verified_at_not_before_collected_at`, which `Collected` gives to each table itself in `__init_subclass__`, so that a model which says `__table_args__` keeps it; that the two times are compared as text, which is the order of the moments; that nothing holds the day of publishing to the moment of collecting, because a page dated the 26th in New Zealand is read on the 25th by the clock of UTC; and that SQLite refuses to delete a source that records came from, with nothing deleted along with it. Tables made for the tests prove the set, in a store the migrations built, so the source they refer to is the real table; the real tables use the set from DAT-011 on. Cut out on 2026-11-25 (PLAN-027): the index over `source_id` (DAT-045) and the rule that names a table of collected records without the set (DAT-046). No table, no migration, no new package, no existing test changed. The planner's trial has 117 new tests and detects 68 of 68 new planted defects and 23 of 23 of DAT-043's.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. The table of a model that is a subclass of `Collected` has the key, the columns the model names, the eight columns of the set in their order and the two times, in the model and in the real SQLite file. `Collected` itself is no table, and the real schema is as it was.
  2. Each of the eight is of its type and of its list; only `published_on` may be without a value; no column of the set gives itself a value, and none is a key, unique or indexed.
  3. A record is stored with all eight and read back as it was given, and in the file it is text in the store's forms. Every value of each of the three lists is stored as its text and read back.
  4. A record without one of the seven that must be said is refused by SQLite, through the model and past it. The day of publishing may be unknown.
  5. A record of a source that is not there is refused. A source that records came from cannot be deleted and its key cannot be changed, in either of two tables of collected records; one whose records are gone can be deleted.
  6. An address that is not a web address, text that is not on the list of its column, and a day of publishing that is not a day are refused by SQLite with the name of the check; a value of another list and a time given for the day are refused before anything is written.
  7. A record last seen at its source before it was collected is refused with the name of the check, through the model and past it, also when the two times were given in different offsets; the same moment and every later one are taken. A record that is seen again says when and keeps when it was collected.
  8. A change that breaks the set is refused, in seven kinds, and the row stays as it was.
  9. A model that says more of its table keeps what it said and has every check of the set; every table has a check of its own; the tables break none of the rules every table keeps.
  10. No existing test changed, and all of them pass. The type check passes with no `ignore` and the lint with no `noqa`.
  11. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates and 1811 tests (backend tests 1181); on the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.

### DAT-045 – An index for the records of one source
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** SQLite finds the records that came from one source without reading a whole table, in every table of collected records.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-25 (PLAN-027), cut out of DAT-008, as the index of the runs was cut out of their table (PLAN-024, DAT-042). One word in `Collected`: the column `source_id` says `index=True`, so every table of a model of collected records has an index over that column, which the naming rule names `ix_<table>_source_id`. Without it SQLite reads the whole of every such table to find the records of one source: whenever the store asks what it holds of a source, and by itself every time a source is to be deleted. Settled in the planner's trial: the index is over the source alone and does not go on with the moment a record was last seen. Five things asked about the records of one source (which came from it, how many, whether any, which were not seen since a moment, and the taking away of its records) are each answered by this index in one step, and no code asks for more yet; an index over two columns would be paid for in every table of collected records at every writing, so the task whose code first asks such a question reads SQLite's plan with real numbers of rows and widens the index if it is worth it. The index is said on the column and not in `__table_args__`, so that a model which says `__table_args__` itself keeps it. It is not unique and is over all rows. SQLite's own account of the deleting of a source (`EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN`) names the look into each table that refers to a source, so the tests read from SQLite itself that this look goes by the index, on every machine the gates run on. Two tests of DAT-008 held that such a table has no index, one of the model and one of the file; both now hold this one, and no other existing test changes. No real table has the set yet, so there is no migration. The planner's trial has 25 new tests and detects 16 of 16 new planted defects and 90 of 90 of DAT-008's and DAT-043's.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Every table of a model that is a subclass of `Collected` has one index over `source_id` alone, named `ix_<table>_source_id` by the naming rule, not unique and over all rows, in the model and in the real SQLite file; no other column of the set has an index.
  2. A model that gives its table an index of its own keeps it and has the index of the set; every table has an index of its own; the tables break none of the rules every table keeps.
  3. SQLite's own account of five things asked about the records of one source is one step each, by the index.
  4. SQLite's own account of the deleting of a source names the look into each table of collected records by that table's index, and no step of it reads a whole table.
  5. Without the index each of the five, and the deleting of a source, reads the whole table; with it and without it the store gives the same answers and refuses the same deleting.
  6. Two existing tests change, the two of DAT-008 that held such a table to have no index, and no other; all tests pass. The type check passes with no `ignore` and the lint with no `noqa`.
  7. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates and 1836 tests (backend tests 1206); on the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.

### DAT-046 – A table of collected records without its provenance is named
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** A table of collected records that was made without the set of DAT-008, or with a part of it changed, is named in plain words, and a test holds the real schema to it.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-25 (PLAN-027), cut out of DAT-008, as the rule of the two times was cut out of them (PLAN-019, DAT-032). Nothing in DAT-008 stops a model from being a subclass of `Base` where it should be one of `Collected`, or from saying a column of the set again in its own way. One new module, `motosphere.db.models.provenance_rule`, with one function, `broken_provenance(schema, not_collected)`, which says in one plain sentence for each break which tables of a schema do not say where their records came from, and one list, `NOT_COLLECTED`, which has the three tables of the real schema that hold no collected records, each with its reason. Settled in the planner's trial: every table holds collected records unless the list names it, so a new table that nobody thought about is named; the list is given to the function, so that the tests give their own; the rule stands beside `broken_rules` and in a module of its own, so neither `base.py` nor `provenance.py` changes; the rule says the set a second time and does not take it from `Collected`, so a change to the set is made in two places and a test fails until they agree; a column is as the set has it by its type and list, by whether a row can be without it, by being given no value, and by what it refers to, and the order of the columns is not held; the index is one over `source_id` alone, over all rows and not unique, whatever it is called; the check is looked for by its name and letter for letter; all missing columns of a table are one sentence, and the index and the check are not asked for where a column they need is missing; the list is held to the schema too, so a name on it that is no table is named, and so is a table on it that has all eight columns. All three real tables are on the list, so a second test takes each off in turn and holds the rule to what it then says. No table, no migration, no new package, no existing test changed. The planner's trial has 74 new tests and detects 86 of 86 planted defects.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `broken_provenance` gives an empty list for the real schema with `NOT_COLLECTED`, and for the tables of models that are subclasses of `Collected`, also one that has rules, a check and indexes of its own.
  2. A table that is not on the list and is without columns of the set is named in one sentence, which names the missing columns in the order of the set and says both ways out.

### DAT-047 – A change is written with its history
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** A field of a record is changed and the change is written to its history in the same unit of work, so that neither exists without the other.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-25 (PLAN-028), cut out of DAT-009. One new module, `motosphere.db.changes`, with one function, `change(work, record, to, run=…)` or `change(work, record, to, decided_by=…)`. It gives a stored record its new values and adds one row to `record_change` for every field that then holds another value, and gives the rows back in the order the fields were given. Settled in the planner's trial: a value is compared, and written to the history, as the text of what the store keeps for it, which the type of the column says as it says it when a row is written, so the history holds the text SQLite itself gives for the column before and after; a column of text takes text and a column of whole numbers a whole number, and what is merely like one is refused, where SQLite would have made something of it; a number with a fraction and bytes have no text yet and a change to such a column is refused, because no real table has one and the choice matters for coordinates; a field that holds the value already gets no row, and when no field differs the record is not touched and the clock is not read; the clock is read once for one call, and every row of the call and the record's `updated_at` carry that moment; `id`, `created_at` and `updated_at` never go through the function; the columns that say where a collected record came from go through it like any other field, so a collector gives the new value and its new provenance in one call and the history holds the address, the day and the moments of the old value (CLAUDE.md §11); a record that is stored for the first time has no history, and a record that was only added is refused until the unit of work has written it; the function writes at once, so what SQLite refuses is refused at the call, the unit of work then fails as a whole, and one that caught the refusal cannot go on; a row of the history is not a record to change; nothing stops a change past the function, and a test stores one so that this is on record. No real table holds records before DAT-011, so three tables made for the tests prove it, in a store the migrations built, with the real table of the changes. ### DAT-048 – A row of the history is neither edited nor deleted
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** What the history said once it says for good: a row of `record_change` is added and never edited or deleted.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-25 (PLAN-028), cut out of DAT-009, which asked that "history rows cannot be edited or deleted through the model". SQLite stops the statement and gives the trigger's sentence: `A row of the history (record_change) is never edited.` before any edit of a row, whatever it would change; `… is never deleted.` before a delete; `… is never replaced.` before a new row whose key a row already has, because `INSERT OR REPLACE` takes the old row out without firing the trigger for a delete, which the trial tried; and `A key of the history (record_change) is 1 or more.` after a new row whose key is below 1, because a new row that names no key shows -1 to the third trigger and a row with that key would stop every new row. The model says the same four statements as text and makes the triggers with the connection that has just made the table, so the test of the models and the migrations, which compares triggers already, holds the two together to the letter of each sentence. A statement that meets no row is not refused. `updated_at` stays, as every table has it, and never moves in this table. A refusal takes back the unit of work it is in. The rows that were there before the migration are held like any other. No table, column or index changes; no new package; `change` and the tests of DAT-009 and DAT-047 are unchanged and pass. A new store is at schema version 6.

  5. The same holds through the model, in seven ways, and on a connection that the store's code did not open.
  6. A new row is still stored, through the model and with plain SQL, and gets the next key; a key that is free may be named. A statement that meets no row is not refused and changes nothing.
  7. A refusal takes back the whole unit of work it is in.

### DAT-049 – Indexes for the history of a record
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** The history of one record, and the changes one collection run caused, are found without reading the whole table of the changes.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-25 (PLAN-028), cut out of DAT-009. Settled in the planner's trial, by asking SQLite for its plan: SQLite ends every entry of an index with the key of its row, so the changes of one record stand in the index in the order they were made, and oldest first, newest first and only the last change are each one search with nothing sorted; `field` as a third column breaks that order and the history is sorted after it is found, so it is not in the index; the key before the table does the same for one record and cannot find the changes of one table; a partial index of a run is used the same and saves little, so the index is plain; the changes one person decided get no index, because nothing asks for them; the plans are the same with 100,000 rows and after `ANALYZE`, which nothing runs. No function that reads the history is added: nothing calls one yet, so the tests write eight questions with the model, as the store's code will ask them, and read SQLite's plan for the statement each really sends. Making an index writes no row, so the triggers that keep the history from being edited do not fire and the rows are as they were. No table, column, check or trigger changes; no new package. Without the indexes each plan is `SCAN record_change`.
  5. SQLite's plan for deleting a run, and for giving a run another key, searches the history by the index of a run and reads no table whole; without the indexes it reads the whole history. Both statements are still refused for a run that caused changes.

- **Objective:** The store has a table of review items: what is in doubt about a record and why, and whether a person has decided it, how, who and when.
- **Description:** Cut down in the planner's trial on 2026-11-26 (PLAN-029) to the table of the review items: that one doubt is one item is DAT-050, the function that opens an item is DAT-051, and the function that decides one, with a decided item keeping its decision, is DAT-052. One new table, `review_item`, with its model `ReviewItem` and the two lists `ReviewKind` (`possible duplicate`, `sources disagree`, `value outside its rules`) and `ReviewState` (`open`, `accepted`, `rejected`) in `motosphere.db.models.review_item`, made by the eighth migration, `make_the_review_items`; the schema is at version 8. One row is one doubt about one record, which is named as the history names it, by the name of its table and its key there. What else an item names goes with its kind: a possible duplicate names the other record of the same table, which has the greater key, so that a pair is said in one way and no record is its own duplicate; the two other kinds name the field, the source that says what it should hold, the address at which the source says so, and what it says, kept as the text it was and never run or followed. Every item says why in plain words, and may have a note, which never leaves the store. An open item says neither who decided nor when; an accepted or rejected item says both. Settled in the planner's trial: the records and the sources of an item are columns and not tables of their own, so that the table itself can hold an item to what its kind needs, with one check for each of the five columns; the three kinds are in one table; the moment of a decision is a column of its own, because a note added later moves `updated_at`; the index of the source, `ix_review_item_source_id`, comes with the table, because SQLite looks for the items of a source whenever a source is deleted, and the test of DAT-045 that reads that plan fails without it. The table holds no collected records and is on the list `NOT_COLLECTED` with its reason. Nothing opens or decides an item yet, the same doubt can still be stored twice and a decided item can still be put back to open: two tests put the last two on record, and the three tasks after this one change that. That the note is in no published file is held by PUB-005. No new package. The planner's trial has 108 new tests and detects 123 of 123 planted defects, and 407 of 407 of the earlier tasks whose files change.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. The eighth migration makes the table `review_item` and the index `ix_review_item_source_id` in a store at version 7 and leaves a source, a row of history, the three indexes and the four triggers as they were; it fails, with the store left at version 7, where a table of its name is there already, and where an index of its name is, with the table it had made taken back. A new store is at schema version 8.
  2. The model `ReviewItem` and the migration say the same table and index; in the file the table is strict, has the columns in the order of the model, one reference (to `source.id`), one index and seventeen checks.
  3. An item of each of the three kinds is stored and read back with every column, and a new item is open; an accepted and a rejected item say by whom and when; a note is kept on an item in any state.
  4. The table refuses: a kind or a state that is not on its list; an item without its kind, the table or the key of its record, its reason or its state; an empty name of a table, of a field or of a person, an empty reason and an empty note; a key of a record that is not above 0; and a value of the wrong kind for a column.
  5. A possible duplicate is refused without the other record, with an other record whose key is not the greater, and with a field, a source, an address or what a source says. An item of each of the two other kinds is refused without any one of those four, and with another record.
  6. The source of an item must exist, and where it says so is a web address; a source that an item names can neither be deleted nor have its key changed, one that none names can be deleted, and SQLite's plan for deleting a source searches the items by the index and reads no table whole.
  7. An open item that says who decided or when is refused, and so is a decided item without either.
  8. What a source says is kept as the text it was, empty text, markup and a statement of SQL among them.
  9. `review_item` is on `NOT_COLLECTED` with a reason of its own, and the real schema breaks neither the rules every table keeps nor the rule of DAT-046.
  10. Two existing tests get an addition each and no other changes; all tests pass. The type check passes with no `ignore` and the lint with no `noqa`.
  11. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates and 2201 tests (backend tests 1571); on the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.

### DAT-050 – One doubt is one review item
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** The store itself refuses a second review item for a doubt it already holds.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-26 (PLAN-029), cut out of DAT-010, which asked that "opening the same doubt twice gives one item". A possible duplicate is the same doubt when it is about the same two records of the same table (`ix_review_item_record_table_record_id_other_record_id`, over the rows that name another record); a doubt about a field is the same doubt when it is about the same field of the same record and the same source says the same thing of it (`ix_review_item_record_table_record_id_field_source_id_source_value`, over the rows that name a field). Settled in the planner's trial: a decided item counts, so that a person is not asked again what they have answered; the kind does not make another doubt between the two that are about a field, nor do the address, the reason or the note; what a source says is compared as the text it is, letter for letter; one unique index over all six columns refuses nothing, because SQLite counts two missing values as two different values, so each shape has its own index over its own rows; an index knows its rows by a column that only they have and not by their kind, because SQLite then answers "is this doubt there already?" by the index for a statement that does not name the kind, which the tests hold by reading SQLite's plan; a store that holds a doubt twice already is not given the migration, which fails and leaves it at version 8 with neither index; a statement that says `INSERT OR REPLACE` is not refused, which belongs to keeping a decision (DAT-052). The indexes of the open items and of all the items about one record are not made here: nothing asks for them yet. No column, check or table changes; no new package.   4. The same possible duplicate is refused a second time, whether the first is open, accepted or rejected, and the first is as it was.   6. A possible duplicate and a doubt about a field of the same record are both stored.

### DAT-051 – Opening a possible duplicate
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** One function puts two stored records that may be the same thing into the review queue, and a doubt that is there already stays one item.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-26 (PLAN-029) as "Opening a review item", cut out of DAT-010, which said: "One function opens an item". Cut down in the planner's trial on 2026-11-26 (PLAN-030) to the possible duplicate; the function that opens a doubt about a field is DAT-053. `open_possible_duplicate(work, record, other, reason=...)` in a new module, `motosphere.db.review`, is what the collecting calls when two records may be the same thing and it cannot tell. It is given the two records themselves and takes the name of their table and their keys from them, as `change` does (DAT-047); it takes them in either order and names the one with the smaller key as the record, which is the one way the table says a pair. One statement looks for an item of that table and those two keys, answered by the index of DAT-050 in one search; if there is one, it is given back as it is and nothing is written, whatever its state, and the reason given the second time is not read; if not, one open item is written to the unit of work and given back. Settled in the planner's trial: one function for each shape of doubt, because the two take different things; only records that are stored and that this unit of work read or stored are taken, as with `change`, and the function does not write what is pending by itself; rows of the tables on the list `NOT_COLLECTED` (DAT-046) are refused, because the queue is for what the collecting cannot settle about what it collected; the refusals are said in the order stored, two tables, the list, one record twice; an empty reason is refused by the store's own check at the call; the item is part of the unit of work and written to it before the function returns; and no other connection can store the same doubt between the look and the write, because SQLite keeps nothing another connection writes while a unit of work that has read is open, which a test tries on every run. No table, column, check, index or migration changes; no existing test changes; no new package. The planner's trial has 54 new tests and detects 45 of 45 planted defects.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `open_possible_duplicate(work, record, other, reason=...)` stores one open item of the kind "possible duplicate" that names the table and the two keys of the records and the reason, and nothing else, at one reading of the clock, and gives it back.
  2. The two records can be given in either order: the smaller key is the record and the greater the other.
  3. The same doubt again gives back the item that is there and stores none, as before and turned round, in a later unit of work and in the same one, and also where the item was accepted or rejected: not one column of it is another afterwards, and the reason given the second time is not read.
  4. A pair that shares one record with another pair, and the same two keys in another table, are other doubts and are stored.
  5. The item is written to the unit of work before the function returns and is kept only when the unit ends; a unit of work that fails leaves no item. The records are not touched and the history gets no row.
  6. Refused before anything is written, each with its one sentence: a record that is not stored or not of this unit of work, as the record and as the other; two records of two tables; two rows, and one row twice, of each table that holds no collected records; and one record twice.
  7. An empty reason is refused by the store at the call, and the unit of work fails as a whole.
  8. The function sends one look, answered by the index of the pairs in one search, and a write only where the doubt is new. Another connection that tries to store the same doubt between the look and the write is told "database is locked".
  9. No existing test changes; all tests pass. The type check passes with no `ignore` and the lint with no `noqa`.
  10. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates and 2297 tests (backend tests 1667); on the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.

Cut down in the planner's trial on 2026-11-26 (PLAN-031) to the function; that the store itself keeps a decision is DAT-054. `decide(work, item, decision, by=..., note=...)` in `motosphere.db.review` is how a decision is written: accepted or rejected, by whom, and when. It gives nothing back. Settled in the planner's trial: the moment is not given but read from the store's clock, once, and is both when the item was decided and when it was last changed; the person is given by name, kept as given, and the table's own checks refuse an empty name and a decision by nobody; a note given takes the place of the note the item has, and without one that note stays; an item that was decided is refused whatever is given for it, the same decision again as well, because that would give the decision another person and another moment; the refusals are said in the order not stored, decided, no decision, so that who gives open for a decided item is told that a decision is kept; a decision is exactly one of the two values of the list, and text that is spelled like one is refused; only an item that is stored and that this unit of work read or stored is taken, by a rule written out in the function with a sentence of its own; a decision is not written to the history of the changes, because the item is itself the record of it and the table of the items holds no collected records; and the function sends one statement and does not read the item again, because SQLite keeps nothing another connection writes while a unit of work that has read is open, which a test tries on every run. No table, column, check, index or migration changes; one existing test changes, the one that holds the list of what the module gives; no new package. The planner's trial has 43 new tests and detects 50 of 50 new planted defects and 101 of 101 of the two earlier tasks whose module it adds to.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `decide(work, item, decision, by=..., note=...)` changes exactly four columns of an open item: its state, who decided, when it was decided and when it was last changed; and its note where one is given. The two moments are one reading of the store's clock. Both decisions are taken, for both shapes of item, and no other item is touched.
  2. Without a note the note of the item stays; a note given is stored in its place, as it was given. The name is kept as it was given.
  3. An item that was decided is refused with its sentence, whatever is given for it, in a later unit of work and in the same one: not one column is another, and the clock is not asked.
  4. Anything but one of the two decisions is refused with its sentence: open, text that is spelled like a decision, nothing, a value of another list.
  5. An item that is not stored or not of this unit of work is refused with its sentence, and nothing is decided. An item stored earlier in the same unit of work is decided.
  6. An empty or blank name, a decision by nobody and an empty or blank note are refused by the store at the call, and the unit of work fails as a whole.
  7. The decision is written to the unit of work before the function returns and is kept only when the unit ends; a unit of work that fails leaves the item open. Nothing but the item is touched, and the history gets no row.
  8. The function sends one statement, which changes the item by its key. Another connection that decides the item just before that statement is told "database is locked".
  9. One existing test changes, the one that holds the list of what the module gives; all tests pass. The type check passes with no `ignore` and the lint with no `noqa`.
  10. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates and 2404 tests (backend tests 1774); on the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.
- **Tests required:** 43 new integration tests in 15 functions, in a store the migrations built, on a real SQLite file, with the real table of the review items, every column of every item read back with plain SQL; they count the readings of the clock and the statements the function sends, and have another connection decide the item just before the function's statement; every existing test, one of them changed. Seventeen planted defects by the builder and the planner's own, those of DAT-051 and DAT-053 among them, because their module changes; the full run of all 19 gates; the workflow run on the pull request, on both machines; the run for `main` after the merge, with its deployment and the check of the published site.

### DAT-054 – A decided review item keeps its decision
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** The store itself keeps a decision on a review item: an item that was decided is neither edited nor deleted, whoever writes to the table.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-26 (PLAN-031), cut out of DAT-052. DAT-010 asked that "a decided item keeps its decision and cannot be reopened silently". Since DAT-052 the function `decide` refuses an item that was decided, but a statement written past it could still change or delete the item. Cut down in the planner's trial on 2026-11-27 (PLAN-037) to what two triggers hold: a row of `review_item` whose state was not open is not changed, in any column, and not deleted. They are made by a tenth migration, `keep_the_decision_of_a_review_item`, and by the model alike, as the triggers of the history are (DAT-048). They ask what the item was before the statement, so an open item is changed, decided and deleted as before, and `decide` is unchanged. The note of a decided item is kept with the rest. A store that holds decided items already is given the triggers and nothing else. That a decided item is not replaced (`INSERT OR REPLACE`) is DAT-055. The schema goes to version 10. No column, check or index changes.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. The schema is at version 10. The tenth migration makes the two triggers `tr_review_item_decided_never_edited` and `tr_review_item_decided_never_deleted` on `review_item` and nothing else; a store at version 9 that holds an open and a decided item keeps both as they were, and the decided one is held from then on. Where a trigger of either name is there already the migration fails, and the store stays at version 9 with neither kept.
  2. No column of a decided item, accepted or rejected, is given another value: each of the sixteen columns is tried on both, and the store says "A decided review item (review_item) is never edited." A change that changes nothing, a change told to pass over what is refused, and a statement over many items that meets a decided one are refused the same, and no item is another afterwards.
  3. A decided item is not deleted, alone or with others: "A decided review item (review_item) is never deleted."
  4. An open item is changed and deleted as before. It is decided with plain SQL and through `decide` as before, and from then on it is kept. A new item is stored open or decided as before.

  8. ### DAT-056 – A key of the review queue is 1 or more
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** The store itself refuses a key below 1 for an item of the review queue, for a new item and for one that is given another key.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-27 (PLAN-040), cut out of DAT-055 in the planner's trial of it. To keep a decided item from being replaced, the store asks before every new row whether a decided item has the key of that row. A new row that names no key has none until it is written, and SQLite shows -1 for it until then; a decided item with the key -1 would so stop every new item that names no key. The history of changes holds its keys to 1 or more for the same reason (DAT-048). Two triggers on `review_item`, `tr_review_item_key_positive` (after a new row) and `tr_review_item_key_stays_positive` (after a row was given another key), refuse a key below 1 with "A key of the review queue (review_item) is 1 or more." They are made by an eleventh migration, `hold_the_key_of_a_review_item_to_1_or_more`, and by the model alike. Both ask after the row is written, when a new item has the key the store gave it. The second fires only for a statement that names the key, so an item that has a key below 1 already can still be changed and given a key of 1 or more; the migration reads and changes no row. Of a decided item the store says first that it is never edited. The schema goes to version 11. No column, check or index changes, and nothing of `motosphere.db.review`.

The eleventh migration makes the two triggers on `review_item` and nothing else; a store at version 10 that holds an item with a key below 1 and one above keeps both as they were, and the first can still be changed and given a key of 1 or more. Where a trigger of either name is there already the migration fails, and the store stays at version 10 with neither kept.
  2. A new item with a key below 1 is refused, open or decided, of either kind, also where the statement says `OR REPLACE` or `OR IGNORE`, and in a queue without items.
  3. A new item that names no key is given the next one as before, with plain SQL and through the model, and a named key of 1 or more is taken as before.
  4. An open item is not given a key below 1, and is given another key of 1 or more as before. A statement over several items that would give one of them such a key changes none.
  5. Of a decided item the store says first that it is never edited.
  6. The refusal stops the statement and no more; in a unit of work the unit fails as a whole.
  7. The refusal holds through the models, and on a connection the store's code did not open, with foreign keys off. A store made from the models has the same four triggers and refuses the same; the test of the models and the migrations passes unchanged.
  8. Two existing tests change: the list of the migrations gets its eleventh name, and the test of DAT-054 that held the table to its two triggers asks for the two of a kept decision by their names. No other existing test changes, and all pass. The type check passes with no `ignore` and the lint with no `noqa`.

### DAT-055 – A decided review item is not replaced
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** The store itself refuses a statement that would put another row in the place of a decided review item.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-27 (PLAN-037), cut out of DAT-054; it stands on DAT-056 (PLAN-040). Since DAT-054 a decided item is neither edited nor deleted. But `INSERT OR REPLACE`, `REPLACE` and `UPDATE OR REPLACE` take out the row their new row clashes with, by its key or as the same doubt (the two indexes of DAT-050), and SQLite fires no trigger for a row it takes out in that way. Two triggers on `review_item`, `tr_review_item_decided_never_replaced` (before a new row) and `tr_review_item_decided_never_replaced_by_another` (before an open row is changed), ask whether a decided item has the key the row would have, or is the same doubt, and refuse with "A decided review item (review_item) is never replaced." They are made by a twelfth migration, `keep_a_decided_review_item_from_being_replaced`, and by the model alike. A trigger cannot know what the statement says is to be done with a clash, so the store refuses whatever it says: a plain `INSERT` that clashes with a decided item is told this sentence where it was told that of the index, and `OR IGNORE` and `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, which would have left the decided item alone, are refused too. An open item is replaced, changed, decided and deleted as before. The key of a new row is asked from 1 on, because a row that names no key shows -1 until it is written (DAT-056). The schema goes to version 12. No column, check or index changes, and nothing of what `motosphere.db.review` does: one sentence of a comment there.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. The schema is at version 12. The twelfth migration makes the two triggers on `review_item` and nothing else; a store that holds items keeps them as they were. Where a trigger of either name is there already the migration fails, and the store stays at version 11 with neither kept.
  2. A new item that has the key of a decided item, or is its doubt (the same two records, or the same thing said of the same field by the same source), is refused, whatever the statement says is to be done with a clash, and every item is as it was.
  3. An open item is not given the key of a decided item and is not made its doubt, in the same sentence, whatever the statement says.
  4. A statement over several rows that would replace a decided item on its way changes no item, also where the statement itself stored or made the decided item.
  5. A new item that is another doubt than every decided item, by one column, is stored as before, and an open item is made such a doubt as before. An open item is replaced, changed, decided and deleted as before. A new item that names no key is stored as before, also in a store where a decided item has the key -1.
  6. Of a decided item that is edited the store says that it is never edited.
  7. The refusal stops the statement and no more: on a plain connection what was written before it and after it is kept, and what a statement over several rows had done before it came to the decided item is taken back by SQLite itself. In a unit of work the unit fails as a whole.
  8. The refusal holds through the models, and on a connection the store's code did not open, with foreign keys off and with SQLite's recursive triggers off or on. A store made from the models has the same six triggers and refuses the same; the test of the models and the migrations passes unchanged.
  9. `open_possible_duplicate`, `open_field_doubt` and `decide` do and say what they did: their tests are unchanged and pass.
  10. Six existing tests change, in four files, as the plan says; no other existing test changes, and all pass. The type check passes with no `ignore` and the lint with no `noqa`.
  11. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates, with 2083 backend tests, and 151 tests more in all than `main` gives at the commit the branch starts from (2890 when that is `c8e4ba4`); on the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.
  12. Only the twelve files under "Scope" of the plan changed.
- **Tests required:** 147 new integration tests in 21 functions, in a store the migrations built, on a real SQLite file, with two open and two decided items written past the model and every column of every item read back with plain SQL after each refusal: a new item in the place of a decided one in eleven ways with four kinds of statement, and with `ON CONFLICT`; an open item put in its place in six ways with three kinds of statement; statements over several rows, in a unit of work and where nothing but SQLite takes them back; another doubt by one column; an open item replaced and changed as before; the model; a connection made with Python's own `sqlite3`, with recursive triggers off and on; a store made from the models. 3 new tests of the twelfth migration, from a store at version 10 that holds a decided item with the key -1, and one case more of the test that tries every migration alone. Planted defects in the triggers, the model and the migration, and those of DAT-054 and DAT-056 again. The full run of all gates on both machines.

### DAT-011 – Countries table
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** The store holds countries, so that everything else can say where it is.
- **Description:** Narrowed on 2026-11-27 (PLAN-042): the country of a source is DAT-057. The table `country`, its model `Country` in `motosphere.db.models.country`, and the thirteenth migration, `make_the_countries`. Settled in the planner's trial. The columns: `alpha_2` and `alpha_3`, the two codes of ISO 3166-1, and `name`, the English short name as its source writes it; then the eight columns of a collected record (DAT-008). The countries are the first real table of collected records, with the index `ix_country_source_id` (DAT-045) and under the rule of DAT-046, whose list is unchanged. A code is held to its form by a check (`ck_country_alpha_2_two_capitals`, `ck_country_alpha_3_three_capitals`): SQLite's `GLOB` with one class of the 26 capital letters for each letter, and a second question for a character with the number 0, which `GLOB` does not read past. Neither the model nor the table puts a code into capitals: what a source writes is stored or refused. Each code is unique by itself. The name is text that is not empty, and two rows can hold one name. The table knows the form of a code and not the standard's list, and it has no rows: they come with DAT-012. Not in the table: the number the standard gives a country, and regions (DAT-013). A defect of the documents is put right on the way: the list in `DATABASE.md` of the tables that hold no collected records lacked `review_item`. The schema goes to version 13. The planner's trial has 154 new tests and detects 55 of 55 planted defects.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. The schema is at version 13. The thirteenth migration makes the table `country` and the index `ix_country_source_id` and nothing else; a store that holds sources and review items keeps them as they were. Where a table or an index of its name is there already the migration fails, and the store stays at version 12 with nothing of the migration kept.
  2. A country is stored and read back with its two codes, its name and the eight things that say where it came from. A row cannot be without any of them but the day it was published, and neither the model nor the table gives one a value.
  3. A code is stored in capitals and refused in any other form or length: each of the eighteen texts of the tests for each code is refused by the check of its column, a text with a character of the number 0 among them. The model does not put a code into capitals.
  4. Two countries cannot share a code of two letters, nor one of three, when a country is stored and when one is changed. Two countries can hold one name.
  5. A name is text that is not empty and is stored as it was given.
  6. A country comes from a source that is there; a source that countries came from cannot be deleted; a country can be deleted, and its key is not given again.
  7. The eight columns keep their rules in the real table: a web address, a calendar day, the three lists, and the two times held together.
  8. The countries of one source are found through the index.
  9. `change` changes a country and writes its history; a change the table refuses leaves the country and no history.

### DAT-057 – A source says its country
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** A source can say which country it is of, so that sources can be found by country.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-27 (PLAN-042), cut out of DAT-011; settled in the planner's trial. One column of `source`, `country_id`: the key of a row of `country`, after `switched_on` and before the two times. It may be without a value, because a body for the whole world is of no one country, and of a source that was stored before somebody looked nobody has said yet; the table does not tell the two apart. The foreign key, `fk_source_country_id_country`, says nothing of deleting: a country that a source is of cannot be deleted. An index over the column, `ix_source_country_id`, finds the sources of one country and answers what SQLite asks before a country is deleted. The fourteenth migration, `let_a_source_say_its_country`, is the first that changes a table that is there and holds rows. It builds the table anew, because SQLite puts an added column after all the others and cannot give it a foreign key with a name, and the table would not be as its model makes it: with the foreign keys left on and checked when the transaction ends, the rows are copied aside, the table is dropped, made again with the column and filled from the copy, every source with its key and everything it had and with no country. The highest key the table has given, which SQLite forgets when a table is dropped, is kept and put back, so the key of a deleted source is still never given again. The four tables whose rows point to a source are not touched. The sources and the countries now point to each other, the only two tables that do: a source is stored before its countries and is said to be of one of them afterwards. The schema goes to version 14. The planner's trial has 40 new tests and detects 57 of 57 planted defects.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. The schema is at version 14. The fourteenth migration gives the table `source` the column `country_id`, after `switched_on` and before the two times, with the foreign key `fk_source_country_id_country` to `country (id)`, and makes the index `ix_source_country_id`; nothing else in the store changes.
  2. In a store that holds sources, offers, runs, review items and countries, every source has after the migration what it had before, its key too, and no country; every row of the four tables that point to a source is as it was; the two copies are gone.
  3. The key of a source that was deleted is not given again after the migration, also where the table has no sources left; a table that never gave a key has none recorded afterwards either.
  4. Where a table of the name of either copy or an index of the name of the index is there already, the migration fails and the store stays at version 13, with the table, its rows and its highest key as before.
  5. A source is stored and read back with its country, and without one; neither the model nor the table gives the column a value.
  6. The country of a source must be there, when a source is stored and when it is changed. What is not a whole number is refused; a whole number written as text or with a point is stored as that number.
  7. A country that a source is of cannot be deleted; it can once no source is of it. Two sources can be of one country.
  8. A source can be of a country that came from it; the two are stored and taken away in the order the tests walk.
  9. The sources of one country are found through the index, and SQLite uses it before a country is deleted.
  10. The model and the migration agree: the test of the models and the migrations passes unchanged. The sources and the countries are the only two tables that point to each other, and the schema breaks no rule.

### DAT-012 – The countries reference file
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** The list of the countries of ISO 3166-1 is in the repository, unchanged from a source whose licence allows it, with that source and its licence recorded, and a reader gives its countries or refuses the file whole.
- **Description:** Narrowed on 2026-11-27 (PLAN-044) from "Countries reference data": registering the source is DAT-058 and DAT-059, and loading is DAT-059. The planner's trial found the source and read its licence: the project iso-codes of Debian, whose file `data/iso_3166-1.json` is the list of the standard, 249 entries, under LGPL-2.1-or-later (copyright 2016 Dr. Tobias Quathamer, as the release's `REUSE.toml` states). The licence permits copying and passing on; it asks that a copy keeps the notice and comes with the licence, that a changed copy says so, and that a work which takes over part of the list is passed on under the same licence. The file and the licence of release 4.20.1 are in `backend/src/motosphere/reference/iso-codes/`, unchanged, with a note of where they came from; the release was fetched once, as its makers published it, from the Ubuntu archive, because the project's own server could not be reached. Every entry's code of three letters and number agree with what Unicode's Common Locale Data Repository gives for its code of two letters. A reader, `motosphere.reference.countries`, gives the countries of such a file, each with its two codes and its name as the file writes it, in the file's order; it reads those three values of an entry and nothing else, and refuses a damaged file whole with a sentence that says what is wrong. `DATA_SOURCES.md` has the source, its licence, what was checked and what else was looked at; ISO's own page could not be read and nothing is said of its terms. Nothing is fetched while the pipeline runs, and nothing is in the store yet.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `backend/src/motosphere/reference/iso-codes/` holds three files and no other: the countries file and the licence, each the file of release 4.20.1 of iso-codes byte for byte, and a note that names both with their SHA-256, the release, the notice of copyright and the licence as the release states them, where the release was fetched, and that the files are unchanged.
  2. `DATA_SOURCES.md` has the source in its registry and a section with what it is, what is taken, its licence as read and what it asks, how it was fetched, what was checked, what else was looked at, and why the source has no category yet.
  3. `countries_of_the_project()` gives the 249 countries of the file in the file's order, each with its code of two letters, its code of three and its name as the file writes it.
  4. `read_countries` reads an entry's two codes and its name and leaves unread whatever more the entry holds.
  5. It refuses, each with a sentence of its own: content that is not UTF-8; that cannot be read as JSON; that has a word JSON does not have; that is nested too deeply; that has a name twice in one place; that is not one list under the name `3166-1` and nothing else; a list without countries; an entry that is not names and values; an entry without text under one of the three names.
  6. It refuses a code that is not two, or three, of the 26 capital letters and nothing else, and one code in two entries.
  7. It refuses a name that is empty, that has white space around it, that has white space inside other than a plain space, or that has a control character.
  8. A file with one wrong entry gives no country at all.
  9. Nothing is fetched, no row is written, and no existing file of the pipeline's code changes; no package is added; no existing test changes; all tests pass.
  10. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates, with 2420 backend tests, and 143 tests more in all than `main` gives at the commit the branch starts from (3362 when that is `08c6ebb`); on the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.
  11. Only the ten files under "Scope" of the plan changed.

It is not `reference data`, which is a text of the list of the kinds of information: a source of the kind `reference list` offers `reference data`. DESIGN.md §7.1 names the twelve and is not changed; the thirteenth is this project's own, and `DATABASE.md` and `DATA_SOURCES.md` say so. ### DAT-060 – The source of the countries can be put in the store
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** One function gives the row of the source of the countries in the store, and stores that row first when the store does not have it: once, as `DATA_SOURCES.md` describes the source, and switched off.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-27 (PLAN-049), cut out of DAT-059; settled in the planner's trial. A new module, `motosphere.reference.countries_source`, says what the store is told of the project iso-codes: its name, its website, its kind (`reference list`, DAT-058), how it is collected from (`public dataset`), its licence (`LGPL-2.1-or-later`), what the licence asks in three sentences, that it has no page of terms, and that it offers `reference data`. `source_of_the_countries(work)` looks for a source of that name. Where there is none it stores one as described, with its offer, and writes both before it returns. Where there is one as described it gives it as it is and writes nothing: what people have said of it (whether its terms were read, whether it is switched on, how often it is looked at, which country it is of) is not looked at, and neither is what else it offers. A source of the name that is described otherwise, in one of the six described columns or because it does not offer `reference data`, is refused with a `CountriesSourceError` whose sentence names everything that differs, and is left as it is: the code does not write over what the store holds, and does not load countries under a description that is not its own. A new row is switched off and says that nobody has read its terms. The planner read the licence of iso-codes, and `DATA_SOURCES.md` says what it found; the column says when a person last read a source's terms, and no person has. The source need not be switched on for its countries to be loaded, because nothing is fetched from it: its file is in the repository. The owner switches it on, or leaves it, after reading the licence. Nothing calls the function yet; no model, migration or command changes. The planner's trial has 38 new tests.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `motosphere.reference.countries_source` has `NAME`, `WEBSITE`, `LICENCE`, `USAGE_RESTRICTIONS`, `DESCRIBED`, `OFFERS`, `CountriesSourceError` and `source_of_the_countries`.
  2. In a store without a source called `iso-codes`, the function stores one row as described, switched off, with its terms read by nobody, no hours and no country, and one offer, `reference data`.
  3. Called again, in the same unit of work or a later one, it gives the same row and writes nothing.
  4. A source of the name that is as described is given as it is: what people said of it stays, and it may offer more.
  5. A source of the name that differs in one of the six described columns, or does not offer `reference data`, is refused with the sentence, which names everything that differs in the order of the table, and nothing is changed.
  6. The source and its offer are kept or taken back with their unit of work.
  7. Every column of a source is described, for people to say, or the store's own; the note beside the file names the same website and licence.
  8. No model, migration, command or existing test changed.
  9. The five documents say what the task did, and that the source is switched off with its terms read by no person.
  10. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates, with 2479 backend tests, and 38 tests more in all than `main` gives at the commit the branch starts from (3428 when that is `70af414`).
  11. On the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) at the first attempt, and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.

### DAT-059 – The countries are loaded into the store
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** One function adds to the store every country of the reference file that the store does not have, each naming the source and the release it came from, and changes nothing else: run again, it writes nothing.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-27 (PLAN-044), cut out of DAT-012; narrowed on 2026-11-27 (PLAN-049) to the loading, where the source is DAT-060 and the command is DAT-061; settled in the planner's trial (PLAN-050). A new module, `motosphere.reference.countries_load`, says which release of iso-codes the file is from (`RELEASE`, 4.20.1), the day it was published (`PUBLISHED_ON`, 2026-01-01) and the address it was fetched from (`ADDRESS`, the file of the release at the Ubuntu archive), as the note beside the file has them. `load_countries(work, countries)` is handed what the file says, asks for the source of the countries (DAT-060), reads the clock once and goes through the countries. A country is known by its code of two letters. One the store has neither by its code of two letters nor by its code of three is added. One the store has with the same code of three and the same name is left as it is and not touched. One the store has with another code of three or another name, or whose code of three the store has under another code of two, is left as it is and named; so is a country of the store that the file does not have. A new row names the source, the address, the moment of the loading as when it was collected and last seen, the day of the release, `public dataset`, `high` and `independent`. The function gives back four lists of codes of two letters (`CountriesLoaded`: `added`, `as_they_were`, `said_otherwise`, `not_in_the_file`). A loading is not a collection run: a run is one attempt to collect from one source, and nothing is collected here, because the file was fetched once, by hand (DAT-012), and is part of the project. That is also why the source need not be switched on for its countries to be loaded (DAT-060). A record is changed with its history, and a change names what caused it: a collection run, or the person who decided it (DAT-047). A loading is neither, and no cause is made up for it. So the loading adds the countries the store does not have and does nothing else: a country the store says something else of than the file, and a country of the store that the file does not have, are left as they are and named to whoever called the function. The store and the file can come to say different things of a country in two ways only: a person changed the store by hand, or a newer release was taken into the repository. Either way a person is at hand to decide which is right. Nothing calls the function yet; no model, migration or command changes. The planner's trial has 40 new tests.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `motosphere.reference.countries_load` has `RELEASE`, `PUBLISHED_ON`, `ADDRESS`, `CountriesLoaded` and `load_countries`.
  2. In a store without countries, `load_countries` with the countries of the project's file stores 249 rows in the order of the file, each with its two codes and its name as the file writes them, and gives them back as added.
  3. Every new row names the source of the countries, `ADDRESS`, one moment of the loading as when it was collected and last seen, `PUBLISHED_ON`, `public dataset`, `high` and `independent`.
  4. A second loading of the same countries sends SQLite no statement that writes, and gives every country back as it was.
  5. A country the store has with another name or another code of three, and a country whose code of three the store has under another code of two, is not added and not changed, and is named in `said_otherwise`. A name is compared letter for letter.
  6. A country of the store that the file does not have is left as it is and named in `not_in_the_file`, in the order of the codes.
  7. A loading stores no collection run, no row of the history and no item of the review queue.
  8. The rows are written when the function returns, and are kept or taken back with their unit of work. A source that is described otherwise, and a list that has one code twice, refuse the loading, and nothing is kept.
  9. The release, its day and its address are the ones the note beside the file names.
  10. No model, migration, command or existing test changed.
  11. The five documents and the note beside the file say what the task did, and that a loading adds and changes nothing.
  12. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates, with 2519 backend tests, and 40 tests more in all than `main` gives at the commit the branch starts from (3487 when that is `403d1fd`).
  13. On the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) at the first attempt, and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.
  14. Only the nine files under "Scope" of the plan changed.

### DAT-062 – What the file says otherwise of a country waits in the review queue
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** Where the store and the reference file say different things of a country, the loading puts what the file says to the review queue, one item for each field the two differ in, and still changes no country.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-27 (PLAN-051). "Use review queues when information conflicts." (CLAUDE.md §11). The loading of the countries finds such conflicts: the store has a country with another name or another code than the file. Since DAT-059 it names the country to whoever called it and keeps the conflict nowhere. PLAN-050 gave as the reason that a person is at hand whenever the store and the file come to differ. That does not keep the conflict: what a command says scrolls away, and the administration that will list what waits for a decision (Phase 10) reads the review queue. The queue has a function for exactly this, `open_field_doubt` (DAT-053): what a source says of a field of a stored record, kept once, and not asked again once a person has answered. So the loading puts what the file says to the review queue, one item for each field the two differ in: the code of three letters or the name of the stored country, or, where the store has the file's code of three under another code of two, the code of two letters of that country. It still changes no country and deletes none, and what it gives back is unchanged. A country of the store that the file does not have is no doubt, because the file says nothing of it. `load_countries` gathers the countries the store says otherwise of as before and, when there is one, hands them to a helper, `_put_to_the_review_queue`, which reads the stored countries that have one of their codes and opens the items with `open_field_doubt`, in the order of the file and for one country in the order code of three, name, the other country: kind `sources disagree`, the source of the countries, the address of the release, what the file says, and a reason in one sentence such as "Release 4.20.1 of iso-codes has another name for the country TR." An item the queue holds already, open or decided, is given back as it is, so a second loading writes nothing. What follows from a decision, that a country gets what the file says, is done by no code yet. No model, migration or command changes, and nothing of the review queue itself. The planner's trial has 16 new tests and changes two existing ones, which said what DAT-059 did and this task changes.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. A country the store has with another code of three than the file opens one item of the review queue about that country: the field `alpha_3`, and the file's code of three as what the source says.
  2. A country the store has with another name than the file opens one item about that country: the field `name`, and the file's name. A name is compared letter for letter.
  3. A country of the file whose code of three the store has under another code of two opens one item about the country that has the code of three: the field `alpha_2`, and the file's code of two.
  4. Every item is of the kind `sources disagree`, open, names the source of the countries and `ADDRESS`, and has the reason in the words of the plan.
  5. The items are opened in the order of the file, and for one country in the order code of three, name, the other country.
  6. No item is opened where the store is as the file has it, for a country that is added, or for a country of the store that the file does not have.
  7. A second loading of the same countries sends SQLite no statement that writes: an item that is there, open or decided, is not opened again and not changed.
  8. A file that says yet another thing of the same field opens an item beside the one that is there.
  9. No country is changed or deleted, and `load_countries` gives back what it gave back before.
  10. The items are kept or taken back with their unit of work.
  11. No model, migration or command changed, and nothing of `motosphere.db`. Of the existing tests, only the two the plan names changed.
  12. The five documents and the note beside the file say that what the file says otherwise waits in the review queue.

### DAT-063 – A command is given the store only when it is ready
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** One function gives a command the data store the settings name, and only when the store is there and at the newest schema. It makes no file and gives the store no migration, and it says in one sentence what is wrong.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-27 (PLAN-052), cut out of DAT-061. The two commands that use the store are about the store itself: `migrate` makes it and brings it to the newest schema, and `schema` looks. The command that loads the countries (DAT-061) is the first that works on what is in the store, and many follow it. Each needs the same thing first: the store as it is, at the schema the code was written for. `open_store` alone does not give that: it makes the file where there is none, and it gives a store that lacks migrations. Nor is it for such a command to migrate the store by itself: a command that was asked for one thing would then do another as well. So one function, `store_to_work_on` in a new module `motosphere.cli.store`, gives a command the store the settings name, and only when it is there and at the newest schema. It makes no file and gives the store no migration. What is wrong is said in one sentence (`StoreNotReadyError`): where there is no file and where the store lacks migrations, in the words the command `schema` has for it, followed by the advice to run `python -m motosphere migrate` first; where `migrate` would refuse the store or it cannot be opened, in the words `migrate` has for it, and with no advice. The function takes the settings, as a command has them, and gives the engine `open_store` made. It is built from `open_store`, `pending` and the `report` of the command `schema`, which all stay as they are. The advice is written out in the module (`MIGRATE_FIRST`), because the module that has the program's name imports every command, and a unit test holds it to that name. Nothing is written, and no connection to the store is left open. No command calls the function yet; the first is DAT-061. No command, model or migration changes.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `motosphere.cli.store` has `store_to_work_on`, `StoreNotReadyError` and `MIGRATE_FIRST`.
  2. Where the settings name no file, `store_to_work_on` raises `StoreNotReadyError` with "There is no data store: … does not exist. Run `python -m motosphere migrate` first.", and neither the file nor its folder is made.
  3. A store that lacks migrations is not given: the error says how many it lacks, its version and the newest, in the words of `schema`, and then the same advice. The store is not given the migrations.
  4. A store `migrate` would refuse, a file that is no database and a folder in the store's place are refused with what `migrate` says of them, word for word, in one line and with no advice.
  5. A store at the newest schema is given, opened as `open_store` opens it, and work on it is kept.
  6. A relative path of the store is taken from the current folder.
  7. After a refusal the file is byte for byte as it was and nothing is made beside it. No connection to the store is left open, refused or given.
  8. `MIGRATE_FIRST` names the command with the command line's own name for the program.
  9. No command, model, migration or existing test changed.
  10. The four documents say what the function gives, what it refuses and in which words.
  11. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates, with 2552 backend tests, and 17 tests more in all than `main` gives at the commit the branch starts from (3520 when that is `297d95d`).
  12. On the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) at the first attempt, and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.
  13. Only the seven files under "Scope" of the plan changed.

Every test of a refusal compares the file byte for byte. 1 new unit test of the advice. Planted defects in the module. The full run of all gates on both machines.
