### DAT-061 – A command loads the countries
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** One command, `python -m motosphere countries`, loads the countries of the reference file into the store, and says what the loading found.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-27 (PLAN-049), cut out of DAT-059; made exact on 2026-11-27 from the planner's trial. The command takes no argument. It is given the store by `store_to_work_on` (DAT-063), reads the reference file that comes with the pipeline, and calls the loading (DAT-059, which puts the source of the countries in the store first, DAT-060) in one unit of work. Nothing is fetched. It says on standard output what the loading found. The first line is always there and adds up: "Of the 249 countries of release 4.20.1 of iso-codes, 249 were added to the data store … and none was there already.", with the whole path of the store. A second line names, by their codes of two letters, the countries the store says something else of, and says that they were left as they are and that what the release says waits in the review queue (DAT-062); a third names the countries of the store that the release does not have. Each is there only when there is such a country. The exit code is then 0, also where the store says something else of a country: the command did what it was asked, and what follows is a person's decision. Run again, the first line says "none was added … and 249 were there already", and nothing is written; the command says what the loading found, not what it wrote, and that nothing was written is held by the tests. A store that is not ready, a damaged countries file and a source that is described otherwise are refused with the error's own sentence on standard error and the exit code 1 (`EXIT_NOT_LOADED`), and the store is left as it was. No other error is caught. The help of the command line gets wider with the longer name, so three existing tests of the help no longer count the blanks between a command's name and what it does. What the published data must say of the licence is PUB-003.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `python -m motosphere countries` is a command of the command line, with a line in the help and a help of its own, and takes no argument.
  2. On a store at the newest schema with no country in it, the command ends with 0, says "Of the 249 countries of release 4.20.1 of iso-codes, 249 were added to the data store … and none was there already.", and the store then has the 249 countries of the file and the one source they name.
  3. Run again, it says "none was added … and 249 were there already", and the file is byte for byte as it was.
  4. A country the store does not have is added, and the first line counts it.
  5. A country the store says something else of is named in a second line, is left as it is, and what the file says of it waits in the review queue. The exit code is 0.
  6. A country of the store that the release does not have is named in a third line and left as it is.
  7. A store that is not ready, a damaged countries file and a source described otherwise each give their own sentence in one line on standard error, nothing on standard output, the exit code 1, and a store that is byte for byte as it was.
  8. The loading is one unit of work: where it fails in the middle, nothing of it is in the store.
  9. No model, migration, or other command changed, and of the existing tests only the three of the help that the plan names.
  10. The six documents say what the command does, what it says and what it refuses.
  11. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates, with 2588 backend tests, and 36 tests more in all than `main` gives at the commit the branch starts from (3556 when that is `1e34fee`).
  12. On the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) at the first attempt, and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.
  13. Only the fourteen files under "Scope" of the plan changed.

### DAT-013 – Regions table
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** The store holds the regions of a country (a state, a county, a province), each of one country and by its name, so that a track can say where in its country it is.
- **Description:** Narrowed on 2026-11-27 (PLAN-054): the code of ISO 3166-2 is DAT-064, and a region inside a region is DAT-065. The table `region`, its model `Region` in `motosphere.db.models.region`, and the sixteenth migration, `make_the_regions`. Settled in the planner's trial. The columns: `country_id`, the country the region is a part of, which cannot be without a value and refers to `country`; and `name`, text that is not empty, stored as its source writes it. A region is a collected record (`Collected`), the second real table of them, so the eight columns that say where a row came from, their checks, the reference to `source`, the index `ix_region_source_id` and the check of the two times come with that, and `NOT_COLLECTED` is unchanged. No two regions of one country have one name (`uq_region_country_id_name`, over the country and then the name); two countries can each have a region of a name; two names are one name only if they are the same text, and the table does not judge whether two names mean one region. A country that has regions cannot be deleted: the reference says nothing of deleting, and SQLite deletes nothing by itself. SQLite keeps an index for the rule of the names, with the country first, and uses it to find the regions of one country, also when a country is to be deleted, so the table has no index of its own over the country; a test holds what SQLite says it uses. The table has no rows and no reference rows: a region comes with the record that needs it.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. The schema is at version 16. The sixteenth migration makes the table `region` and the index `ix_region_source_id` and nothing else; a store that holds sources, countries and what points to them keeps them as they were. Where a table or an index of its name is there already the migration fails, and the store stays at version 15 with nothing of the migration kept.
  2. A region is stored and read back with its country, its name and the eight things that say where it came from. A row cannot be without any of them but the day it was published, and neither the model nor the table gives one a value.
  3. A region is a part of a country that is there, when it is stored and when it is moved. A country that has regions cannot be deleted; one without regions can, and so can a country whose regions were taken away first.
  4. Two regions of one country cannot have one name, when a region is stored, when it is given another name and when it is moved. Two countries can each have a region of a name. Two names are one name only if they are the same text.
  5. A name is text that is not empty and is stored as it was given.
  6. A region comes from a source that is there; a source that regions came from cannot be deleted; a region can be deleted, and its key is not given again.
  7. The eight columns keep their rules in the real table: a web address, a calendar day, the three lists, and the two times held together.
  8. The regions of one source are found through `ix_region_source_id`, and the regions of one country through the index of the rule of the names; SQLite reads no table whole when a country or a source is to be deleted.
  9. `change` changes a region and writes its history; a change the table refuses leaves the region and no history.
  10. The model and the migration agree: the test of the models and the migrations passes unchanged. The whole schema with the regions breaks neither the rules every table keeps nor the rule of where records came from, whose list is unchanged.
  11. One existing test changes, the list of the migrations' names; no other existing test changes, and all pass. The type check passes with no `ignore` and the lint with no `noqa`.
  12. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates, with 2705 backend tests, and 117 tests more in all than `main` gives at the commit the branch starts from (3685 when that is `a8b30bb`); on the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) at the first attempt and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.
  13. Only the eleven files under "Scope" of the plan changed.
- **Tests required:** 104 new integration tests in 32 functions, in a store the migrations built, with one source and two countries in it, on a real SQLite file, through the model and past it as plain SQL, each refusal with what SQLite said: what is stored; what a region cannot be without; the country that must be there, when a region is stored and when it is moved; a country with regions, without them and after they were taken away; one name in one country and in two, when a region is stored, renamed and moved; names that differ in a capital, a space or an accent; names that are empty; the source; the key of a deleted region; the rules of the eight columns; the two indexes, as SQLite says it uses them; the history. 9 new unit tests of the model. 3 new integration tests of the sixteenth migration, on a store that holds sources, countries and what points to them, and one case more of the test that tries every migration alone. Planted defects in the model and in the migration. The full run of all gates on both machines.
- **Tests executed:** By the tester thread, against the planner's test plan (shared folder, `testing/DAT-013/`), in a clean checkout of pull request #214 merged with `main` (Linux, with the two variables for the browser set): the full run, 19 of 19 gates passed, "Tests: 3703 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped.", exit code 0 (repository 82, script tests 219, backend 2705, frontend unit 406, browser 291). The planner's 45 planted defects, one at a time, in the model and the migration of the pull request, each against the 413 tests of eight files (baseline 413 passed): 45 of 45 detected, each by the same tests as in the planner's trial. The plan's 13 prove-it steps: each fails the tests the plan names (8, 9, 6, 2, 3, 5, 95, 110, 1, 7, 2, 2 and 3 tests). By hand, in a store of the tester's own: `countries` without a store says that there is none and to run `migrate` first, with the exit code 1; `migrate` makes a store at schema version 16, and `schema` says it is up to date at version 16; `countries` adds 249 of 249; the table `region` is there, `STRICT` and without rows; with foreign keys on, a region of a country that is not there is refused, a second Canterbury in New Zealand is refused by the rule of the names, Canterbury in New Zealand and in Australia are both taken, and New Zealand cannot be deleted while it has a region. ### DAT-064 – A region says its code of ISO 3166-2
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** A region can say the code the standard ISO 3166-2 gives it, such as `NZ-CAN`, so that a region is known across sources that write its name differently.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-27 (PLAN-054), cut out of DAT-013, and narrowed on 2026-11-28 (PLAN-056): that the code agrees with the country of the region is DAT-067, and what the code means for the rule of the names is DAT-066. The column `iso_3166_2` of `region`, in the model `Region` and by the seventeenth migration, `let_a_region_say_its_code`. Settled in the planner's trial. The column may be without a value: not every region a source names has a code, and a region is not kept out of the store for lacking one. A code is held to the form of the standard by the table itself (`ck_region_iso_3166_2_form`): two capital letters, a hyphen and one, two or three capital letters or digits, and no character with the number 0. The form was measured before it was written: all 5046 codes of the list of ISO 3166-2 that comes with release 4.20.1 of iso-codes have it (327 with one character after the hyphen, 3099 with two, 1620 with three). Nothing is put right: a code in small letters or with a space is refused, not changed. No two regions have one code (`uq_region_iso_3166_2`), whatever their countries; SQLite takes rows without a code for different, so any number of regions can be without one, and it keeps an index for the rule that finds the region of a code, so the column has no index of its own. SQLite puts a column that is added in place after all the others and cannot give it such a rule, so the migration builds the table anew, as the fourteenth did the sources: every region keeps what it had and its key and gets no code, the highest key the table gave is kept, and the index over `source_id` is made again. No table of a store at version 16 points to a region, so the migration does not ask SQLite to wait with its check of what points where. No codes are loaded: which codes there are is what a source says.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. The schema is at version 17. The seventeenth migration builds the table `region` anew with the column `iso_3166_2` after the name, the check `ck_region_iso_3166_2_form` and the rule `uq_region_iso_3166_2`, and makes the index `ix_region_source_id` again; of all the tables and indexes of the store only `region` is written otherwise afterwards, and no table of the migration's own is left.
  2. Every region that was there has all it had, its key too, and no code. The highest key the table gave is kept: the key of a deleted region is not given again, also in a table with no regions left and in one that never had one. Every row a region points to is as it was, and no pointer is broken.
  3. After the migration the regions are held to their country, their source, the rule of the names and the check of the name as before.
  4. Where a table of the name of either copy is there, and where the index cannot be made at the migration's end, the migration fails and the store is as it was, at version 16.
  5. A region is stored and read back with its code. A region may be without one, and any number of regions may be; neither the model nor the table gives one.
  6. A code is two capital letters, a hyphen and one, two or three capital letters or digits. Every other text is refused by the table, when a region is stored and when it is given a code later, through the model and past it. Nothing is put right.
  7. No two regions have one code, when a region is stored and when it is given a code later. A code is free again once the region that had it is deleted, has another or has none.
  8. The region of a code is found through the index SQLite keeps for the rule, and the column has no other index.
  9. `change` gives a region a code, another and none, and writes its history; a change the table refuses leaves the codes and no history.
  10. The model and the migration agree: the test of the models and the migrations passes unchanged. The whole schema breaks neither the rules every table keeps nor the rule of where records came from.
  11. Eight existing tests change, as the plan names them: the list of the migrations' names, and seven tests of the regions that name the columns, the constraints or the indexes of the table. None loses an assertion; no other existing test changes, and all pass. The type check passes with no `ignore` and the lint with no `noqa`.
  12. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates, with 2821 backend tests, and 116 tests more in all than `main` gives at the commit the branch starts from (3860 when that is `728105c`); on the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) at the first attempt and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.
  13. Only the ten files under "Scope" of the plan changed.
- **Tests required:** 104 new integration tests in 20 functions, in a store the migrations built, with one source and four countries in it, on a real SQLite file, through the model and past it as plain SQL, each refusal with what SQLite said: what is stored; a code of each of the six forms the standard has; thirty-eight texts that are not of the form, when a region is stored and when it is given the text later; bytes and numbers; one code in two regions, when a region is stored and when it is given a code later; regions without a code; a code that is free again; the history; the column in its place; the index, as SQLite says it uses it. 3 new unit tests of the model. 8 new integration tests of the seventeenth migration, in 6 functions, on a store that holds sources, a country and regions with a gap in their keys, and one case more of the test that tries every migration alone. Planted defects in the model and in the migration. The full run of all gates on both machines.
- **Tests executed:** By the tester thread, against the planner's test plan (shared folder, `testing/DAT-064/`), in a clean checkout of pull request #221 merged with `main` (Linux, with the two variables for the browser set): the full run, 19 of 19 gates passed, "Tests: 3903 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped.", exit code 0 (repository 82, script tests 219, backend 2821, frontend unit 477, browser 304). The plan's 16 prove-it steps: each fails the tests the plan names (83, 11, 25, 59, 81, 10, 157, 4, 2, 2, 1, 4, 2, 7, 1 and 196 tests). ### DAT-068 – The key of a review item stays 1 or more under every name of the key
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** No statement can give a review item a key below 1, whichever of the names SQLite has for the key of a table the statement uses.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-28 (PLAN-056), a defect of DAT-056 that the planner found while trying DAT-067; made exact on 2026-11-28 from the planner's trial. The trigger `tr_review_item_key_stays_positive` fired after an update of the column `id`. SQLite has three more names for the key of a table, `rowid`, `_rowid_` and `oid`, and a statement that sets the key by one of them does not fire a trigger that names the column: of thirteen ways to name the key that the new tests try, a key below 1 was refused under the three spellings of `id` and taken under the ten others. No store holds a review item, because nothing writes one yet (Phase 10), so no record is wrong. The trigger is written anew, in the model and by the eighteenth migration, `hold_the_key_of_a_review_item_under_every_name`: it fires after every update of a review item, whatever the statement names, and refuses a key below 1 that is not the key the item had (`WHEN NEW.id < 1 AND NEW.id != OLD.id`). So an item that had a key below 1 before the eleventh migration can still be given a note, and a key of 1 or more, and cannot be given another key below 1. The migration takes the old trigger out and makes the new one; it changes no table, no index and no row, puts no item right, and fails, with the store left at version 17, where the trigger it takes out is not there. The eleventh migration is not changed. The first trigger of the keys, for a new item, fires whatever the statement names and is as it was; no other trigger of the store names a column. The documents of DAT-056 are put right.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. The schema is at version 18. The eighteenth migration takes the trigger `tr_review_item_key_stays_positive` out and makes it anew, to fire after every update of a review item and to refuse a key below 1 that is not the key the item had. Every table, index and other trigger of the store is written as it was, and every row is as it was.
  2. An open item cannot be given a key below 1 by `id`, `rowid`, `_rowid_` or `oid`, in other capitals or in the marks SQLite takes around a name, alone or with other columns in the same statement, by a query, or by an item that is stored again. After each refusal every item is as it was.
  3. An open item can be given a key of 1 or more under each of these names, and the key it has.
  4. An item that had a key below 1 before the rule keeps it, can be given a note, can be given a key of 1 or more and then cannot go back, and cannot be given another key below 1.
  5. A new item is held as before, also where its key is named by one of SQLite's own names. A decided item is told that it is never edited, under these names too.
  6. The refusal takes back a unit of work, and holds on a connection the store's code did not open and in a store made from the models.
  7. Where the trigger the migration takes out is not there, the migration fails and the store is left at version 17.
  8. The model and the migration agree: the test of the models and the migrations passes unchanged. The eleventh migration is unchanged.
  9. One existing test changes, the list of the migrations' names; it loses no assertion. No other existing test changes, and all tests pass. The type check passes with no `ignore` and the lint with no `noqa`.
  10. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates, with 2965 backend tests, and 144 tests more in all than `main` gives at the commit the branch starts from (4047 when that is `100f8d0`); on the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) at the first attempt and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.
  11. Only the eight files under "Scope" of the plan changed.
- **Tests required:** 137 new integration tests in 14 functions, in a store the migrations built, on a real SQLite file, past the model as plain SQL and through a unit of work, each refusal with what SQLite said and every item read back afterwards: a key below 1 under thirteen ways to name the key; eight kinds of statement under SQLite's own three names; a key of 1 or more under each name; the key named twice; the key an item has; a new item under SQLite's own names; an item stored again; a decided item; a unit of work; a bare connection; a store made from the models; and an item that had a key below 1 before the rule, in a store that was at version 10 when it was stored. 6 new integration tests of the eighteenth migration, in 4 functions, on a store at version 17 that holds three open items, one of them with a key below 1, and one case more of the test that tries every migration alone. Planted defects in the model and in the migration. The full run of all gates on both machines.

### DAT-067 – The code of a region agrees with its country
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** The store itself refuses a region whose code begins with the two letters of another country, so that the code of a region can be trusted to say which country it is in.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-28 (PLAN-056), cut out of DAT-064; follows DAT-068, whose finding it uses; made exact on 2026-11-28 from the planner's trial. A code of ISO 3166-2 begins with the code of two letters of the region's country, and the table held the form of a code only: it would take `AT-1` for a region of New Zealand. The letters of a country are a column of another table (`country.alpha_2`), which a check of `region` cannot read, so four triggers hold the two together, in the model `Region` and by the nineteenth migration, `hold_the_code_of_a_region_to_its_country`: `tr_region_code_of_its_country` and `tr_region_code_stays_of_its_country` on the regions, for a region that is stored, given a code or moved to another country, and `tr_country_code_of_its_regions` and `tr_country_code_stays_of_its_regions` on the countries, for a country that is given other letters or takes the place of another (a statement that says `REPLACE` puts a country in the place of the one that had its key, and SQLite fires the trigger for a new row then and none for the row it takes out). Each fires after the row is written and asks of the row as it now is; none names a column (DAT-068), so each fires after every change of a row of its table. The store says "The code of a region (region) begins with the code of its country (country)." or "The codes of the regions of a country (region) begin with its code (country)." and takes the statement back. Both letters are compared, as text. A region without a code is asked nothing: it is a part of any country, and a country whose regions have no codes can be given any letters. What the tables themselves refuse (the form of a code, one code for one region, one set of letters for one country), they refuse first, in their own words. The migration makes the triggers and changes no table, no index and no row; it puts nothing right, because it cannot know whether the code or the country of a region is wrong: a region that was wrong before the rule stays as it is and can then be put right or deleted and nothing else, and until then every other change of it and of its country is refused. No real store holds a region yet. The migration fails, with the store left at version 18, where a trigger of one of the four names is there already.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. The schema is at version 19. The nineteenth migration makes the four triggers `tr_region_code_of_its_country`, `tr_region_code_stays_of_its_country`, `tr_country_code_of_its_regions` and `tr_country_code_stays_of_its_regions`. Every table, index and trigger that was there is written as it was, and every row is as it was.
  2. A region whose code begins with the two letters of its country is stored, through the model and past it. One whose code begins with other letters is refused with the sentence of the regions, whatever the statement says is to happen on a clash, and after each refusal every row is as it was.
  3. A region is not given a code that begins with other letters than its country's, whether it had a code or none; it is given a code of its country, and none.
  4. A region with a code is not moved to another country; one without a code is; a region is moved together with a code of the new country, in one statement.
  5. A country whose regions have codes is not given other letters, and not by a country that takes its place; a country whose regions have no codes is given any letters. Each refusal says the sentence of the countries.
  6. A region without a code is a part of any country. A region and a country are changed in every other way as before, and deleted as before.
  7. The form of a code, the rule of one code and the rule of one set of letters for a country are refused in their own words, before the new rule.
  8. A refusal takes the statement back and not the work before it; in a unit of work it takes the work back. The rule holds on a connection the store's code did not open, and in a store made from the models.
  9. On a store that was at version 18 and holds a region whose code is of another country, the migration changes no row; the region can then be put right or deleted, and until then every other change of it and of its country is refused.
  10. Where a trigger of one of the four names is there already, the migration fails and the store is left at version 18.
  11. The model and the migration agree: the test of the models and the migrations passes unchanged.
  12. One existing test changes, the list of the migrations' names; it loses no assertion. No other existing test changes, and all tests pass. The type check passes with no `ignore` and the lint with no `noqa`.
  13. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates, with 3220 backend tests, and 255 tests more in all than `main` gives at the commit the branch starts from (4330 when that is `087ff4f`); on the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) at the first attempt and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.
  14. Only the eight files under "Scope" of the plan changed.
- **Tests required:** 245 new integration tests in 43 functions, in a store the migrations built, on a real SQLite file, through the model and past it as plain SQL, each refusal with what SQLite said and the rows read back afterwards: a region that is stored, with a code of its country, of another, and without one, by every kind of statement; a region that is given a code; a region that is moved; a country that is given other letters, that takes the place of another, and that is new; several rows written by one statement; a refusal and the work before it; a unit of work; a bare connection; a region whose country is not there; a store made from the models; the four triggers as SQLite holds them; the plan SQLite makes to find the regions of a country; and a region that was wrong before the rule, in a store that was at version 18 when it was stored. 9 new integration tests of the nineteenth migration, in 3 functions, on a store at version 18 that holds regions with codes, one of them of another country, and one case more of the test that tries every migration alone. Planted defects in the model and in the migration. The full run of all gates on both machines.
- **Tests executed:** By the tester thread, against the planner's test plan (shared folder, `testing/DAT-067/`), in a clean checkout of pull request #237 merged with `main` as it was then (`3a412d9`; Linux, with the two variables for the browser set): the full run, 19 of 19 gates passed, "Tests: 4346 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped." (repository 82, script tests 219, backend 3220, frontend unit 516, browser 309). The planner's 111 planted defects, one at a time, in the model and the migration of the pull request, each against the tests the test plan names (baseline 785 passed): 111 of 111 detected, with no difference from the planner's list of which tests detect which. The tester's run of the list reached its time limit during the last defect; the tester put `history.py` back as it was and ran that defect by itself: detected, as the list says. The plan's 15 prove-it steps: each fails the number of tests the plan names (32, 19, 86, 195, 25, 42, 3, 9, 3, 160, 132, 1, 4, 15 and 172), and the worktree was clean afterwards. Refused then, as the plan says: the codes `AU-MBH` and `AT-9` for regions of New Zealand, the move of a region with a code to Australia, the letters `XN` for a country whose regions have codes, and both changes of the letters of a country while its region Otago held `AT-1`. Taken: `NZ-MBH`; Southland, which has no code, moved to Australia; after Otago was given `NZ-OTA`, both changes of the letters; and then Australia as `XA` with its region as `XA-STL`. ### DAT-066 – Two regions of a country can share a name when their codes tell them apart
- **Status:** IN PROGRESS
- **Objective:** The store can hold both of two regions of one country that have one name, as the standard's own list has them, when their codes tell them apart; of the regions of a country that say no code, no two have one name.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-28 (PLAN-056); made exact on 2026-11-28 from the planner's trial. Measured by the planner on the list of ISO 3166-2 that comes with release 4.20.1 of iso-codes (5046 codes of 200 countries): 36 pairs of regions share a name within one country, as Maputo, the city (`MZ-MPM`) and the province (`MZ-L`) of Mozambique; no name is there three times in a country. The rule of DAT-013, one name in a country, let the table hold one region of each such pair. The rule now holds among the regions of a country that say no code: it was written into the table (`uq_region_country_id_name`) and is an index over the country and the name that holds those regions and no others (`ix_region_country_id_name`, `WHERE iso_3166_2 IS NULL`), in the model `Region` and by the twentieth migration, `let_regions_with_codes_share_a_name`. Two regions that each say a code can have one name, because no two regions have one code (DAT-064). A region with a code and one without can have one name too: whether they are one region, named by two sources, the table cannot know, and it is asked in the review queue by the task that first stores regions ("Use review queues when information conflicts.", CLAUDE.md §11); this task writes no such code. A region cannot lose its code beside one of its name that says none. SQLite says of a refusal what it said of the old rule. The index SQLite kept for the old rule also found the regions of one country, for whoever asks, when a country is to be deleted, and for the rule of DAT-067; an index over some rows cannot, so the country gets an index of its own (`ix_region_country_id`). SQLite cannot take a rule out of a table that is there, so the migration builds the table anew, as the seventeenth did: every region keeps all it had, its code and its key, the highest key given is kept, and what went with the table is made again, the index over the sources and the two triggers of DAT-067 that are on the regions, the triggers after the table is filled, so that a region whose code is not of its country, which DAT-067 left as it was, is left as it is. No region can be in the way of the new rule: every store kept the old one, which asked for more. The migration fails, with the store as it was at version 19, where something of a name it makes is there already. No store holds a region yet.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. The schema is at version 20. The table `region` is written as it was but for the rule `uq_region_country_id_name`, which is gone. The index `ix_region_country_id_name` is over the country and the name of the regions whose code is not there and holds no two alike; the index `ix_region_country_id` is over the country. Every other table, every other index and all fourteen triggers are written as they were.
  2. Two regions of one country that each say a code can have one name, through the model and past it; a region with a code can be given the name of another region of its country.
  3. Two regions of one country that say no code cannot have one name: the second is refused when it is stored, when it is given the name, when it is moved to the country, and when it loses its code beside one of its name that says none. Each refusal says `UNIQUE constraint failed: region.country_id, region.name`, and after it the regions are as they were.
  4. A region with a code and one without can have one name in a country, whichever is stored first.
  5. Two countries can each have a region of one name that says no code.
  6. The regions of one country are found through the index over the country, for whoever asks, when a country is to be deleted, and for the rule of DAT-067; a region without a code is found by its country and name through the index of the rule. SQLite says so in its plans.
  7. On a store at version 19 that holds regions with and without codes, one of them with a code that is not of its country, the migration keeps every region with all it had, its code and its key, keeps the highest key, and breaks no pointer. A region the store refused before is taken after it.
  8. After the migration the regions are held to their country, their source, a name that is not empty, the form of a code, one code for one region and the code of their country, each in the words it had.
  9. Where a table of the name of a copy, or an index or a trigger of a name the migration makes, is there already, the migration fails and the store is as it was, at version 19.
  10. The model and the migration agree: the test of the models and the migrations passes unchanged.
  11. Seven existing tests change, as the plan names them; none loses a thing it held. No other existing test changes, and all tests pass. The type check passes with no `ignore` and the lint with no `noqa`.
  12. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates, with 3269 backend tests, and 49 tests more in all than `main` gives at the commit the branch starts from (4396 when that is `f2fde15`); on the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) at the first attempt and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.
  13. Only the twelve files under "Scope" of the plan changed.
- **Tests required:** 31 new integration tests in 21 functions, in a store the migrations built, on a real SQLite file, with one source and four countries, through the model and past it as plain SQL, each refusal with what SQLite said and the regions read back afterwards, with four pairs the standard's list has (Maputo, Tartu, Cantabria, Dhaka): both regions of a pair; regions that say no code; one with a code and one without; a code that is taken away or given; the index as SQLite has it written, the plan by which a region without a code is found, and a statement that stores a region or does nothing. 16 new integration tests of the twentieth migration, in 8 functions, on a store at version 19 that holds regions of two countries, with and without codes, one of them with a code that is not of its country, and one case more of the test that tries every migration alone. One new unit test of the model, and seven tests that were there changed as the plan names them. Planted defects in the model and in the migration. The full run of all gates on both machines.
- **Tests executed:** –
- **Passed / Failed:** – / –
- **Verification status:** Not verified
- **Commit:** –
- **Plan:** `docs/plans/DAT-066.md`

### DAT-065 – A region inside a region
- **Status:** NOT STARTED
- **Objective:** A region can be a part of another region of its country, as a county is of a state, so that a track can be placed as closely as its source places it.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-27 (PLAN-054), cut out of DAT-013; follows DAT-066 since 2026-11-28 (PLAN-056). One column of `region` that refers to the table itself and may be without a value: a region without a parent is a part of its country only. The migration changes a table that is there. Measured by the planner on the list of ISO 3166-2 that comes with release 4.20.1 of iso-codes: 1456 of its 5046 regions have a parent, always a region of the same country, never the region itself, and no region lies more than two deep. With a parent, the rule of the names as DAT-066 leaves it changes once more: two regions of one country that say no code may have one name when they are parts of different regions (there are counties of one name in many states). The planner's trial settles how SQLite is given that rule where the parent may be without a value, how the table holds that a parent is of the same country, and what is done about a region that would be a part of itself, directly or through others.
- **Acceptance criteria:** a region may be without a parent; a parent is a region that is there, of the same country; a region is not its own parent; two regions without a code under one parent cannot have one name, nor two of one country that have no parent, and two under different parents can; a region that other regions are parts of cannot be deleted; the regions that were there keep everything they had and have no parent; the model and the migration agree.
- **Tests required:** integration tests against a real SQLite file; the migration applied to a store at the version before it that holds countries and regions; planted defects; the full run of all gates on both machines.
- **Tests executed:** –
- **Passed / Failed:** – / –
- **Verification status:** Not verified
- **Commit:** –

### DAT-016 – Tracks table with validated coordinates
- **Status:** NOT STARTED
- **Objective:** The store holds tracks with a position that is checked, and can find the tracks inside a rectangle of the map quickly.
- **Description:** Table, model and migration for tracks: name, country, region where known, latitude and longitude in WGS 84, how exact the position is, disciplines, status (open, closed, unknown), surface and facilities as published, website, the organisation that runs it, and provenance. A position is both numbers or neither. An index serves lookups by bounding box, including boxes that cross the 180th meridian; which kind of index (ordinary or R*Tree) is decided by measurement in the planner's trial.
- **Acceptance criteria:** a latitude outside -90 to 90 or a longitude outside -180 to 180 is refused; one coordinate without the other is refused; a position of exactly 0, 0 is refused (a common sign of a missing value, and open sea); a bounding-box query returns exactly the tracks inside, across the 180th meridian too, and uses the index; no coordinate is ever invented.
- **Tests required:** integration tests against a real SQLite file, with boundary and antimeridian cases and a check of the query plan; a measurement with 100,000 generated positions, used only to choose the index and never stored; the migration applied to a store at the version before it; planted defects; the full run of all gates on both machines.

### DAT-019 – Snapshot: the store written as text files
- **Status:** NOT STARTED
- **Objective:** The whole store can be written out as text files that are the same, byte for byte, whenever the store is the same.
- **Description:** A command writes every table to text files in a folder: one record per line, records and fields in a fixed order, UTF-8, Unix line ends, with the schema version and a list of the files and their hashes, and reports the snapshot's size. Large tables are divided into files by a rule that depends only on the data. This is the form in which the store is kept between scheduled runs (DAT-001's decision), so a change to the store shows as a small, readable difference. The snapshot also holds the last key each table gave (SQLite's `sqlite_sequence`, DAT-029): without it a rebuilt store would give the key of a deleted row again.
- **Acceptance criteria:** writing the same store twice gives identical files, on Linux and on Windows alike; one changed record changes one line; no file comes near GitHub's file size limit, by a margin the plan states; the folder is written completely or not at all; internal notes are included (this is the store, not the published data) and the folder says so.
- **Tests required:** unit tests of the ordering and the encoding; integration tests against a real SQLite file with every table filled, comparing bytes across two writes and across the two machines by hash; planted defects; the full run of all gates on both machines.
- **Tests executed:** –
- **Passed / Failed:** – / –

### DAT-020 – Snapshot: the store rebuilt from text files
- **Status:** NOT STARTED
- **Objective:** A store rebuilt from its snapshot is the same store, and the round trip is proven.
- **Description:** A command builds a new store from a snapshot folder: the schema is brought to the snapshot's version, the records are loaded, and the schema is then brought to the newest version. Every file is checked against its hash first. A snapshot that is damaged, incomplete or newer than the code is refused whole. Every row is stored with the key it had, and every table goes on counting from the last key it gave before the snapshot was made (DAT-029), so that no key is given twice across a rebuild.
- **Acceptance criteria:** store → snapshot → store → snapshot gives identical snapshot files; every table, key and history row survives; a row deleted before the snapshot does not get its key given again after the rebuild; foreign keys hold in the rebuilt store; a changed, missing or extra file is refused in plain words and no store is left behind; a snapshot of an older schema is rebuilt and migrated.
- **Tests required:** integration tests against real SQLite files with every table filled, including a snapshot made at an earlier schema version; damaged snapshots; the two commands run for real; planted defects; the full run of all gates on both machines.

- **Passed / Failed:** – / –

### PUB-001 – Published data layout and manifest of files
- **Status:** NOT STARTED
- **Objective:** The published data has a fixed layout, and the manifest lists every file with its hash, so the browser can tell exactly what it should receive.
- **Description:** The layout of the published folder is decided and documented (`ARCHITECTURE.md` §6): which files exist, how they are named so that a changed file has a new address, and the format version. The manifest gains the list of files with size and content hash, and record counts. The export writes through the store for the first time; with an empty store it publishes the manifest and nothing else. The frontend's manifest reader accepts the new format and still refuses one it does not know.

- **Tests required:** unit tests of the manifest model; integration tests of the export against a real store; frontend unit tests of the reader; browser tests against freshly exported data; planted defects; the full run of all gates on both machines; the run for `main` with its deployment and the check of the published site.

- **Status:** NOT STARTED
- **Objective:** The frontend has one way to fetch published files: checked against the manifest, kept while the page is open, and honest about loading and failure.

The countries file is its first user, shown nowhere yet; the first screen that uses it comes with the track directory.

# Phase 3 – Interactive 3D Earth (GLB)

Written out in full by PLAN-033. The stage in the middle of the screen
gets a real 3D Earth, drawn by the Cesium engine, that a person can turn,
zoom, pan and tilt with a mouse, a keyboard and a finger. It is track B
(see "Two tracks" above): its tasks change the front end (`frontend/`) and
no file of the pipeline, and they follow one another in the order below.

What holds for every task of the phase:

- **The real globe is tested.** Browser tests run on the built site in
  headless Chromium, which draws WebGL in software, on Linux and on
  Windows. A test that says the Earth is shown reads pixels of the
  picture; a test that says the camera moved reads where the camera is.
  A stand-in for the engine is used only in unit tests of the page's own
  logic, where the test environment cannot draw.
- **Nothing is asked of another computer.** Until a provider of imagery
  has had its terms read and recorded (GLB-012), the globe uses only
  files of the site itself. A browser test counts the requests that leave
  the site, and there are none.
- **The globe holds no data.** Markers, tracks and events are not part of
  this phase and are never written into the front end (CLAUDE.md §9);
  they come from the published data, in Phase 4.
- **Plain words on the screen.** Controls say what they do ("Zoom in",
  "North up"), with no words from map software (CLAUDE.md §8).
- A task's details are settled by the planner's trial before its plan is
  written, and a task that proves too large to test thoroughly is split
  then. Two tasks were added when the phase was written out: the old
  GLB-001 is GLB-001 and GLB-022, which follow one another here although
  their numbers do not, and touch screens got a task of their own,
  GLB-023, after tilt.

### GLB-001 – The Cesium engine installed
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** The front end has the Cesium engine as a locked dependency: the packages `@cesium/engine` and `@cesium/core` are named, locked and installed, unit tests use the engine as it is installed, and a repository check holds which packages the site ships.
- **Description:** The package `@cesium/engine` 26.5.0 (CesiumJS 1.147, Apache 2.0) is added to the front end: the engine without the ready-made viewer of the full `cesium` package, which MotoSphere does not use because its controls are its own. The planner's trial found that the engine keeps its arithmetic (positions, angles, the shape of the Earth) in a package of its own, `@cesium/core` 0.1.1, and warns when those names are taken from the engine, which it says it will stop handing out; so the project names that package too, and the engine and the application must use one copy of it. The two bring 28 packages, none with a script at install, and need no newer Node.js than the project asks for since FND-046. Nothing of the application imports the engine yet, so the build makes the same files as before. One new file of unit tests uses the engine as installed; one new file of repository checks holds the list of the packages the site ships.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. `frontend/package.json` names `@cesium/core` (`^0.1.1`) and `@cesium/engine` (`^26.5.0`) under `dependencies`, beside React and `react-dom`, and `npm ci` installs them from the lock file on Linux and on Windows, with no script run at install.
  2. The dependency audit reports no advisory. The type check, the lint, the format check and the build pass with the engine installed.
  3. Unit tests import the engine as installed and get from it the positions the reference ellipsoid WGS84 gives for six places, the order of longitude and latitude, heights in metres and the way back to degrees. A warning from the engine fails a test.
  4. A unit test holds that the engine's globe stands on the very ellipsoid the application gets from `@cesium/core`.
  5. The repository checks hold which packages the site ships; that none is named in both groups; that the lock file names the same packages for the project, installs one copy of each shipped package and does not mark it as only for the work; and that `cesium`, `@cesium/widgets` and `resium` are neither named nor installed.
  6. The check of the least Node.js (FND-046) passes unchanged with the new lock file.
  7. The build makes the same files as before.
  8. No existing test changes; all tests pass.
  9. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates, with 62 repository checks and 223 frontend unit tests, and 20 tests more in all than `main` gives at the commit the branch starts from (2436 when that is `b141ed0`); on the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.
- **Tests required:** 11 new frontend unit tests through the real packages: the reference ellipsoid, six places on the Earth, the order of the degrees, heights, the way back, and the one ellipsoid of the engine and the application. 9 new repository checks in 2 classes, standard library only: the reading of made-up lock files, and six checks of the real package file and lock file. 35 planted defects against the repository checks and 6 against the unit tests. A comparison of the built site with the one `main` gives. The full run of all gates on both machines.
- **Tests executed:** In a clean checkout of pull request #145 (Linux, Python 3.13.18, Node 22.23.4, npm 10.9.9, uv 0.11.32), with nothing installed beforehand: the full run twice. The first run: 18 of 19 gates, "Tests: 2435 passed, 1 failed, 0 skipped.": one browser test this task did not touch, `narrow.spec.ts`, "narrow screen, 360×740 › the data status and the date each have a line", failed with "Expected: 9, Received: -12". The planner found the cause before running anything again: the test measures the top bar before the data has arrived, and the computer was running planted defects of the other track at the time; with the data a second late the same measurement gives -12 every time, on `main` as on the pull request (PLAN-036, FND-048). The second run: 19 of 19 gates passed, "Tests: 2436 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped.", exit code 0 (repository checks 62: 53 before, 9 new; frontend unit tests 223: 212 before, 11 new). 35 planted defects against the repository checks, 35 of 35 detected with the results of the trial, the plan's prove-it steps 1 to 6 among them; prove-it steps 7 to 12 against the unit tests, each with the plan's result. The six files the build makes are the same as on `main`, byte for byte. On GitHub, read by the planner from the runs and job logs: run 45925021461 on the pull request's commit, both gates jobs green at the first attempt, each log ending "19 of 19 gates passed." and "Tests: 2436 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped.", with 62 repository checks and 223 frontend unit tests on both machines (Node.js 22.23.5; `npm ci` added 136 packages on Linux and 133 on Windows), the three jobs after the gates skipped; run 45926591250 for `main` after the merge, all five jobs green at the first attempt, both gates jobs with the same two closing lines, the site republished, and the check of the published site ending "https://bigdaddy284.github.io/MotoSphere/ is the site that was built: 7 of 7 files."
- **Passed / Failed:** 2436 / 0 tests in the second local run and on both machines on GitHub (repository 62, script tests 206, backend 1774, frontend unit 223, browser 171); 2435 / 1 in the first local run, the one failure being the test of FND-048; all 19 gates passed on both machines on GitHub; 35 / 0 planted defects detected and 6 / 0 prove-it steps against the unit tests; the published site: 7 / 7 files identical to the build
- **Verification status:** Verified by the planner on 2026-11-27. All 7 files are identical to the planner's trial. No existing test changed. The failure of the first local run is not this task's: the task changes no file of the application and no browser test, and the cause is in a test that is on `main`; it is FND-048, the next task on track B.
- **Commit:** `6554b04`
- **Plan:** `docs/plans/GLB-001.md`

### GLB-022 – The engine's own files in the built site
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** The files the engine fetches while it runs are part of the built site, under one folder of the site's path.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-27 (PLAN-033), cut out of the old GLB-001; cut to the built site by PLAN-041. The engine does part of its work in background scripts and reads programs, tables and pictures from files of its own, which come with the package in its folder `Build` (277 files with `@cesium/engine` 26.5.0) and are not code the build takes in. A step of the build, `frontend/sitebuild/engineFiles.ts`, puts every file beneath that folder into the site as it is, under the folder `cesium/`, in a set order; none is picked by hand, so that another version of the engine brings what it needs. Two files are left out, each named in the step with its reason: the logos of Bing Maps and of Google Earth, which the engine shows only with imagery of those companies; the build stops when a listed file is not there any more. The site so gets 275 files, 8.3 MB, and has 283 in all. The engine's licence is in the site's `licences.txt` already (FND-047). Nothing of the application asks for a file of the folder yet, so a visitor's browser fetches none. The development server (GLB-024), telling the engine the address (GLB-025) and what GitHub Pages says a `.wasm` and a `.jpg` file are (FND-053) are tasks of their own.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. The built site holds, under `cesium/`, every file the installed engine brings beneath its folder `Build` but the two listed as left out, each byte for byte, and no other file there.
  2. No picture, program or folder of the engine is anywhere else in the built site.
  3. The site under test answers one file of each of the eight kinds at its address under the site's path with the status 200, the type a browser needs and the bytes of the engine; and every one of the files with its bytes.
  4. The two logos, which the installed engine brings, are answered "not found". So are a file the engine does not bring, the rest of the engine's package, its own code, a file of the engine outside the folder, the folder `Build`, the folder of the installed packages, and the folder at the root of the server.
  5. The engine's licence is in the site's `licences.txt`.
  6. The step gives the files in a set order on every computer, leaves out a listed file and no other of the same name, and refuses, each in a plain sentence: a listed file that is not there, a listed folder, an engine that is not installed, a folder without files, and a link among the files.
  7. The list of what is left out has exactly the two logos, each with its reason.
  8. The step runs when the site is built and not in the development server.
  9. No existing test changes; all tests pass; no package is added.
  10. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates, with 320 frontend unit tests and 253 browser tests, and 39 tests more in all than `main` gives at the commit the branch starts from (2791 when that is `26a396c`); on the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.
  11. Only the seven files under "Scope" of the plan changed.
- **Tests required:** 16 new unit tests of the step: on folders made up for the test, with the entries of a folder given in an order that is not that of their names, and on the engine as it is installed. 23 new browser tests that compare the built folder with the installed engine on the disk and fetch every file through the site under test, with the two logos named in the tests themselves. Planted defects in the step against the unit tests and against the browser tests. The full run of all gates on both machines. The deployment and the check of the published site, which then compares 283 files.
- **Tests executed:** In a clean checkout of pull request #165 (Linux, Python 3.13.18, Node 22.23.4, npm 10.9.9, uv 0.11.32), with nothing installed beforehand: the full run, 19 of 19 gates, "Tests: 2791 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped." (frontend unit tests 320: 304 before, 16 new; browser tests 253: 230 before, 23 new). The planner's 51 planted defects in the step: 36 against the 16 unit tests, 36 of 36 detected; 15 against the browser tests of the engine's files and of the licences, with the site built anew for each, 15 of 15 detected. On GitHub, read by the planner from the runs and job logs: run 45972388594 on the pull request, both gates jobs green, each log ending "19 of 19 gates passed." and "Tests: 2791 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped.", with 320 frontend unit tests and 253 browser tests on both machines, the three jobs after the gates skipped; run 45974353239 for `main` after the merge, all five jobs green at the first attempt, both gates jobs ending "Tests: 2791 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped.", the site republished with the engine's files, and the check of the published site ending "https://bigdaddy284.github.io/MotoSphere/ is the site that was built: 283 of 283 files."
- **Passed / Failed:** 2791 / 0 tests locally and on both machines on GitHub for `main` (repository 80, script tests 206, backend 1932, frontend unit 320, browser 253); 2791 / 0 on both machines for the pull request, merged with `main` as it then stood; all 19 gates passed; 51 / 0 planted defects detected.
- **Verification status:** Verified by the planner on 2026-11-27. All 7 files are identical to the planner's trial. No existing test changed, no package was added and the lock file is unchanged. The step gives the same 275 files in the same order on Windows as on Linux. The builder reported the full run with 2791 tests, 283 files in the built site with 275 of them under `cesium/`, the fifteen prove-it steps with the plan's results and no deviation.

### GLB-024 – The engine's own files in development
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** In development the engine's files are answered at the same addresses as in the built site, with the same bytes.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-27 (PLAN-041), cut out of GLB-022; settled in the planner's trial. The step of GLB-022 works when the site is built. `npm run dev` builds nothing: the development server answers from the folder of the project, where the engine's files are not. A second step in the same file, `engineFilesInDevelopment`, answers the folder `cesium/` of the site's path from the installed engine while the development server runs. It goes by the same list as the build (`engineFiles`), so the two cannot differ in which files they hold: each file the built site has is answered, read anew for every request, with its length and the type a browser needs (a table of the eight kinds the engine brings, `SERVED_AS`), and every other address in that folder is answered "not found" by the step itself, the two logos that are left out and a file the project or its folder `public/` has there among them. A name is looked up in the list and never on the disk, so a name that leaves the folder and a name in other capitals than the file has are "not found" on every computer. The server does not start when the engine is not installed, when a listed file is not there, and when the engine brings a file of a kind the table has no type for, which it names. The task spoke of teaching the same step; a step of Vite is for the build or for the server, so it is a second one beside it, and the step of the build and its tests stay as they are. The tests start the development server itself, on this computer only and on a port that is free; the same tests hold that it answers an address that names no file with "not found" and not with the page (FND-052 asked that by hand).
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. With `npm run dev`, every address the built site has under `cesium/` of the site's path is answered with the file of the installed engine, byte for byte, with the status 200, its length and the type `SERVED_AS` has for its kind.
  2. `SERVED_AS` has a type for each kind of file the installed engine brings, and for no other.
  3. Every other address in that folder is answered "not found" with no content: the two logos, a file the engine does not bring, the folder itself, a name that leaves the folder, a name in other capitals than the file has, and a file the project or its folder `public/` has there.
  4. A request that would change something is answered with no file of the engine.
  5. An address outside the folder that names no file is answered "not found" and not with the page; the engine's files are not at the root of the server; the page, the application's code and an icon are answered as before.

  7. The development server does not start, and says why, when the engine is not installed, when a file listed as left out is not there, and when the engine brings a file of a kind without a type.
  8. The step is for the development server only; the step of the build and the built site are unchanged, and no existing test changes.
  9. No package is added; all tests pass.
  10. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates, with 371 frontend unit tests, and 51 tests more in all than `main` gives at the commit the branch starts from (3007 when that is `2802943`); on the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.
  11. Only the seven files under "Scope" of the plan changed.

Planted defects in the step and in the settings. The full run of all gates on both machines.

### GLB-025 – The engine is told where its files are
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** Before the engine is first used, the application tells it the address of its own files, built from the one place that names the site's path.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-27 (PLAN-041), cut out of GLB-022; settled in the planner's trial. The engine asks for its files beneath one base address. Left to itself it works that address out from where its own code was loaded, which in a built site is a file of the build with a name of its own, and not the folder of GLB-022. One module of the application, `frontend/src/globe/engineAddress.ts`, builds the address from the site's path and the name of the folder, both in `frontend/site.ts`, and puts it under the name the engine reads (`CESIUM_BASE_URL`); the build and the development server take the folder's name from the same place. The application tells the engine where it starts, in `main.tsx`, before it shows anything and nowhere else: the engine reads the address the first time it needs a file, and keeps it. The address is a path and names no computer, so the engine asks the site the page came from and nobody else. The engine's own function for this (`buildModuleUrl.setBaseUrl`) is not used: it is not in the engine's type declarations, and calling it needs the engine loaded, which GLB-002 does only when the globe is shown. Nothing of the application loads the engine yet. A test of the engine when nobody tells it was tried and dropped: the engine keeps the address, and the test runner gives a test no second engine.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. One module, `frontend/src/globe/engineAddress.ts`, names the address of the engine's files: the site's path and the folder's name, both from `frontend/site.ts`, and a last stroke.
  2. The address is a path from the top of the site and names no other computer.
  3. The module's function puts the address under the name the engine reads, also over another address.
  4. With it told, the engine as installed builds the address of a background script, of a program, of a table, of a picture of the Earth and of the description of those pictures beneath the folder, on the computer of the page.
  5. Nothing else in the application writes the address or the engine's word for it: not its code, not the page and not the settings of the build. The build and the development server take the folder's name from `frontend/site.ts`.
  6. The application tells the engine in one place, where it starts, before it shows anything and with nothing before it but what it brings in; it has told it by the time it has started, and also when it cannot start.
  7. In a browser, the built page has told the address, and the site answers a file of each of the eight kinds beneath it with the status 200, at the address the site has for it, with the length of the engine's file.
  8. No existing test changes; the built site holds 283 files as before; no package is added; all tests pass.

### GLB-026 – The stage says when the browser cannot show the globe
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** In a browser that gives a page no 3D picture, the globe stage says in two plain sentences that the globe cannot be shown and what a person can do, and the rest of the application works as before.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-27 (PLAN-053), cut out of GLB-002 and GLB-004; made exact on 2026-11-28 from the planner's trial. Started in a browser that gives no 3D picture, the engine puts up a panel of its own and logs an error on the console. With FND-059 that is the browser of every test outside the project of the globe, so the page must know what to do there before the globe is put into it. `browserDraws3D()` in the new module `frontend/src/globe/drawing.ts` asks the browser for a 3D picture of the kind the engine draws with (`webgl2`), on a drawing surface of its own that is not put into the page, and gives the picture back at once (`WEBGL_lose_context`). The new component `GlobeStage` asks once, when it is first shown; where the answer is no it shows two paragraphs in the place of the globe, "The 3D globe cannot be shown in this browser." and why and what a person can do, in the middle of the stage, and where the stage is too low for the words they start at its top and the stage scrolls. The test environment of the unit tests gives no picture to draw on, so a stand-in (`frontend/src/test/drawing.ts`) is put in place before every unit test, as a browser that draws. Nothing of the engine is loaded, and no package is added.

### GLB-027 – The life of the globe over a stand-in engine
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** A `Globe` component starts one engine in its drawing surface when it is shown and stops it when it goes, proven by unit tests with a stand-in for the engine.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-27 (PLAN-053), cut out of GLB-002; made exact on 2026-11-28 from the planner's trial. The component (`frontend/src/globe/Globe.tsx`) owns one element for the drawing surface and is handed how the engine is loaded and started, so that the unit tests can stand in for it: the loading gives a way to start an engine in an element, and starting gives the way to stop it. The component brings in `react` and nothing else. The engine is asked for when the component is shown, not while it is only drawn. React shows a component twice in development (`StrictMode`), and a component can go before the loading is over: in neither case is an engine left running or started for a globe that has gone. A loading that fails, a way of loading that breaks at once and an engine that cannot be started are handed to whoever shows the component, once, with the very thing that went wrong, and only while the globe is shown; what a person is told then is GLB-004. Another listener or another way of loading handed in later starts nothing anew (`useEffectEvent`). The component keeps no state, so it asks React for nothing after it is shown. The stand-in of the tests (`frontend/src/test/engine.ts`) lets each loading end when a test says so, remembers what was started where and how often it was stopped, and comes back from a loading only when what waited on it has run. The component is not put into the page yet, so nothing a user sees changes and the task has no browser tests. No package is added.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. Shown once, the component asks for the engine once and, when it has arrived, starts it once, in its own element, which is in the page then. A globe that is drawn and not shown asks for nothing.
  2. When the globe goes, its engine is stopped once, and not before.
  3. An engine is never started after the globe has gone, and one whose loading ends afterwards is not started.
  4. Under `StrictMode` exactly one engine is running once both loadings have ended, in either order, and it is stopped once when the globe goes.
  5. A loading that fails, a way of loading that breaks at once, and an engine that cannot be started are each told once to the listener the globe has then, with what went wrong, and nothing is running. A globe that has gone tells nothing.
  6. Another listener or another way of loading handed in later neither loads nor starts nor stops anything.
  7. The component brings in `react` and nothing else, and no unit test loads the engine by way of it.
  8. The stand-in does what its tests say, and comes back from a loading only when what waited on it has run.
  9. No existing test changes, and all tests pass. The type check, the lint and the format check pass.
  10. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates, with 477 frontend unit tests and 304 browser tests, and 43 tests more in all than `main` gives at the commit the branch starts from (3787 when that is `7d38a50`).
  11. On the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) at the first attempt, and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.
  12. Only the seven files under "Scope" of the plan changed.
- **Tests required:** 43 new frontend unit tests. 27 of the component: its element; the engine asked for once, and not while the globe is only drawn; started in its own element in the page; nobody told when all goes well; nothing anew for another listener or another way of loading; stopped once when the globe goes and not before; never started after the globe has gone; each of the three failures told once with the very thing that went wrong, to the newest listener, and not for a globe that has gone; shown again; two globes; seven cases under `StrictMode`; and that the component names nothing of the engine. 16 of the stand-in. ### GLB-029 – The globe says when its first complete picture is drawn
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** The `Globe` component tells whoever shows it, once, when its engine has drawn the first complete picture, proven by unit tests with the stand-in for the engine.
- **Description:** Added on 2026-11-28 (PLAN-057), cut out of GLB-002, from the planner's trial. The engine needs a moment after it is started, and two things have to know when the first complete picture is there: the browser tests of the globe (GLB-002, GLB-003), which must not look before it, and what a person is told while the globe loads (GLB-004). An engine is now started with a listener (`Listener`, with `drawn`), which it cannot be started without, and the component can be handed whom to tell (`onDrawn`), which may be left out. The component tells once, though the engine says it again; never for a globe that has gone; the listener it has then, through `useEffectEvent`, so that another listener starts nothing anew; and each showing has its own note of what was told. A listener that fails is not a failure of the engine: what it fails with goes back to the engine. The component still keeps no state and brings in `react` and nothing else. The stand-in of the tests keeps whom each engine was handed and has an engine say it when a test says so (`draws()`), at once, for the engine started first or the one named, stopped or not. The listener is an object so that GLB-004 can have the engine say a second thing, that it failed while drawing. The component is not in the page yet, so nothing a user sees changes and the task has no browser tests. No package is added.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. An engine is started with a listener, and the component can be handed whom to tell that the picture is drawn; it can also be shown without.
  2. When the engine says that its first complete picture is drawn, whoever shows the globe is told once, with nothing else, and the engine goes on running. Nobody is told before the engine says it.
  3. An engine that says it again tells nobody a second time.
  4. Nobody is told what an engine says after its globe has gone.
  5. The listener the globe has then is the one told. Another listener handed in later neither loads nor starts nor stops anything.
  6. Of two globes each is told of its own engine only, and a globe that is shown again after it has gone is told of its new engine only. A globe that React put aside and shows again is told a second time, of its new engine. Under `StrictMode` it is told once, of the one engine that runs.
  7. Nobody is told that a picture is drawn when the engine cannot be loaded or started. A listener that fails is not told to `onFailed`; what it fails with goes back to the engine, which goes on running.
  8. The stand-in has an engine say it when a test says so, at once, for the engine started first or the one named, and names an engine that was not started in a sentence.
  9. The component still brings in `react` and nothing else.
  10. No test of `Globe.test.tsx` changes; in `engine.test.ts` only the calls that start an engine change, and none loses an assertion. All tests pass. The type check, the lint and the format check pass.
  11. `python scripts/check.py` reports 19 of 19 gates, with 505 frontend unit tests and 304 browser tests, and 28 tests more in all than `main` gives at the commit the branch starts from (3815 when that is `386697a`).
  12. On the pull request both gates jobs are green (Linux and Windows) at the first attempt, and the three jobs after the gates are skipped.
  13. Only the seven files under "Scope" of the plan changed.

### GLB-002 – The globe shows the Earth
- **Status:** PASSED – VERIFIED
- **Objective:** Where the browser gives a 3D picture, the globe stage shows the Earth, drawn by the real engine with the pictures that come with it, loaded only from the site itself and after the page is there.
- **Description:** Narrowed on 2026-11-27 (PLAN-053) and on 2026-11-28 (PLAN-057); made exact on 2026-11-28 from the planner's trial. One new module, `frontend/src/globe/earth.ts`, is the real way to start the engine and the only module of the application that brings the engine in: one `CesiumWidget` in the element it is handed, with the Natural Earth II pictures that are among the engine's files in the site, found through the engine's own `buildModuleUrl`, and told to draw only when something has changed. After every picture it asks whether a piece of the Earth is still waiting (`scene.globe.tilesLoaded`); when none is, it stops asking and says that the Earth is drawn, once the engine has finished that picture (`queueMicrotask`), so that whoever is told can stop it there and then. Stopping takes the widget away and gives the browser its 3D picture back (`WEBGL_lose_context`), which the engine's own way of ending does not do. The page hands the application the way to load that module, and fetches it, and the engine with it, when the stage shows the globe: the engine is a script of its own of 3,920 kB (1,041 kB as it travels) beside the page's own 230 kB. The stage shows the `Globe` component in an element that fills it and marks it as busy (`aria-busy`) until the Earth is drawn; a failure is handed to the browser until GLB-004. The unit tests cannot draw and hand the stage the stand-in; the real engine is tested in the browser tests. The engine's own mark in a corner, its sky and its way with the mouse are left as the engine has them (GLB-005, GLB-012, GLB-015). The build's advice that a script is larger than 500 kB is left: the engine is a script of its own already (Phase 13). No package is added.
- **Acceptance criteria:**
  1. In a browser that gives a 3D picture the stage holds one drawing surface, which fills the stage and has a pixel for each pixel of it, and the page does not scroll.
  2. The Earth is drawn with the pictures that come with the engine: when the stage says that the globe is no longer busy, the description of the pictures and at least two of them have arrived from the engine's folder of the site, at that very moment, which the page notes itself. For a second and a half after it no file arrives that had not, no request is under way, the picture of the stage stays the same, and the engine draws at most one picture more.
  3. Every file the page asks for comes from the site, under its path; nothing is asked of another computer.
  4. The page does not wait for the engine: with the script of the engine held back, the top bar, the data status and the sections are there and work, the globe is marked as busy and has no drawing surface; let through, the Earth is drawn. The script is asked for once.
  5. The engine can be stopped and started again in the page twenty times in a row and the browser says nothing: each engine has one drawing surface while it runs and leaves nothing in its element when it is stopped, and the globe of the page still has its picture afterwards. An engine that is left running says once that the Earth is drawn, though it draws again; and whoever is told can stop it there and then.
